# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP14

## S#1. Lena's apartment. Foyer. Evening.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A pre-war apartment on the park side of the city. Cream walls, walnut floors, the apartment Lena has not lived in for nine months and has not redecorated since coming back to it. A square of mail on the floor under the brass slot — the doorman knows by now to leave it where it falls. Lena comes in, slate coat over her arm, necklace at her throat. Noah a half step behind, dark coat unbuttoned. The door closes behind them.

Lena does not turn on the chandelier. She turns on a single table lamp by the foyer mirror. Picks up the small stack of mail.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: foyer, two figures, single lamp
- CU: Lena's hand on the stack of mail
- ECU: the top envelope of the stack — cream paper, no return address, only Lena's name handwritten in a hand she does not yet recognize
- OTS Lena: Noah's profile, watching her hand, not her face

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
You want me to take it.

LENA
I want you to stand there while I open it.

NOAH
The doorman wouldn't have brought up something off-routine.

LENA
The doorman didn't bring this up. This came through the slot. Look at the bottom corner.

She turns the envelope. The bottom corner has a small embossed mark — three stylized birds in flight, no name underneath. The kind of mark a private bank would press into wax, except the wax is gone and the mark is just the impression in the cream paper.

NOAH
(quiet, recognizing)
That's the bank your mother used. Same three birds on every Sterling document I've ever seen.

LENA
That's what I thought.

## S#2. Lena's apartment. Living room. Evening.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The living room — a long sofa, a low marble table, the city through the long window. Lena sits on the edge of the sofa. Noah takes the armchair across from her. He does not crowd. He sets a pair of clean cotton gloves on the table between them — from the pocket of his coat — and a small evidence bag. He always carries both.

Lena does not put on the gloves. She slides one finger under the flap of the envelope. Opens it. Pulls out a single folded sheet of cream paper. Unfolds it.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: living room, two figures, low lamp
- CU: Lena's finger under the envelope flap
- ECU: the page unfolding, three lines of typed text and a fourth handwritten line at the bottom
- OTS Lena: Noah across the table, watching her face for the change

[GRAPHIC/UI]
The page. Centered, typed, three lines:

*Helena's daughter.
The trust was for you. It was always for you.
The third trustee is not who you think.*

Below the typed lines, handwritten, in a hand that is not Helena's — sharper, narrower — one more sentence:

*Find me before she does.*

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(reading, low)
*Find me before she does.*

NOAH
That's not your mother's hand.

LENA
No. That hand belongs to someone who is alive.

A beat. Lena does not put the page down. She does not put it in the evidence bag. She holds it in both hands, flat on her lap.

NOAH
The trust is the one I found nineteen months back. Halcyon. The redacted fifth name is sending you mail.

LENA
The redacted fifth name is asking me to find them. Before Eileen does.

NOAH
*She* in the second sentence is Eileen. *She* in the fourth sentence is also Eileen. The writer is using one pronoun for two roles. They want me to assume both. We should assume both.

LENA
We should assume both.

## S#3. Lena's apartment. Living room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah rises. Crosses to the window. He does not look out. He turns. Stays standing because he is thinking and he does not think well in chairs. Lena has not moved from the edge of the sofa. The page still in her hands.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Noah at the window, half-turned back to the room
- OTS Noah: Lena on the sofa, the page in her lap
- CU: Lena's thumb at the corner of the page, pressing the crease
- ECU: the handwritten line — *Find me before she does* — under the lamp

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Halcyon was founded in 1995. Your mother was pregnant with you that year. She set it up before you were born. She named four trustees that I found and a fifth whose name was sealed in the same year. The fifth name has stayed sealed for thirty-one years.

LENA
My mother set up a trust for me before I was born and put a fifth person on it whose name she wanted hidden.

NOAH
She put a fifth person on it whose name she wanted hidden *from someone specific.* You don't pay the kind of legal fees it takes to seal a name in 1995 unless you are hiding the name from one particular reader.

LENA
Eileen.

NOAH
Eileen, or someone Eileen would have shown the document to. They are in the same orbit. They have been in the same orbit since before you were born.

LENA
Then the fifth name is on my side.

NOAH
The fifth name is sending you mail through a door slot at night. That is not the behavior of someone in a strong position. The fifth name is in trouble.

Lena looks down at the page. Her thumb still at the crease. She lifts the page once into the lamp light to read the handwriting again, then sets it flat on the table — not in the evidence bag.

LENA
My mother died when I was twenty-two. She had eight years to tell me about this trust. She didn't. So one of two things is true. Either she planned to tell me later and didn't get to. Or she planned for me to find out from somebody else, on a specific occasion, and the occasion was something she could anticipate.

NOAH
What occasion could she anticipate.

LENA
The occasion of a woman coming forward claiming to be me.

## S#4. Lena's apartment. Living room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah does not respond for a long second. He crosses back to the chair. Sits this time. He looks at the page on the table — but he does not pick it up. The page is hers. She has not made it his.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Noah sitting, hands clasped between his knees
- OTS Noah: Lena across from him, the page between them
- CU: the page on the marble table, lit from the lamp
- ECU: Lena's fingers at the chain of her pendant, the inscription pressed against her palm

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Your mother anticipated Mara.

LENA
My mother anticipated a Mara. Not necessarily this one. She built a structure that would activate when somebody tried what Mara tried. The structure has now activated. The first thing it produced is this letter.

NOAH
The second thing it will produce is a face.

LENA
The second thing it will produce is the fifth name. Whoever that is.

A beat. Noah looks at the page. Looks at her.

NOAH
Tomorrow morning I'm walking into your mother's bank with this envelope and I'm going to make somebody acknowledge that mail went out from their building. I want to walk in with you next to me.

LENA
I'll be there. Pick me up at eight.

NOAH
There is going to be a person at the end of this. Not a document. Not a wire transfer. A person who knew your mother.

LENA
I know.

NOAH
The person who knew your mother is going to know things about her that you did not get to know. You need to be ready for that.

LENA
I have been ready for that since I was twenty-two.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: the handwritten line on the page in the lamp light — *Find me before she does* — the ink slightly raised, fresh enough that it has not yet fully set.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The letter was fresh. The hand was the hand of someone who had only just decided to send it. Someone had been watching the screen behind Mara last night the same way I had. Someone had been waiting eight years for that screen. And the screen had set them moving before it had set me moving.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(low, to the page on the table)
I am coming. I am coming as fast as I can.

She does not pick the page up again. She lets it stay on the marble in the lamp light. Noah does not move to take it either. They sit across from it together until the lamp clicks itself off on the timer and they are sitting in the city light.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP15

## S#1. Cross Manor. Sitting room. Morning.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The Cross Manor sitting room — not the ballroom, not the front steps, not the boardroom downtown. The room Victoria takes the people she cannot lie to. Walnut paneling, two long windows with the lawn behind them, a small writing desk against one wall, a low fire in the marble fireplace because the morning is grey and Victoria has always burned wood when she means a conversation. Two upholstered chairs across from each other in front of the fire. Between them, a low table with a silver tray — tea service, untouched.

Victoria already seated, baroque pearls at her throat, hair in the severe French twist. Her hands flat on the arms of the chair — not folded in her lap, not at the teacup. Flat on the arms.

Lena comes in alone. Mr. Bannon has been left at the front door at her instruction. Noah is in the car at the curb at his own. Lena wears the slate coat, necklace at her throat. Hair down today — small change, deliberate.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: sitting room, two chairs, low fire, two long windows
- MCU: Victoria seated, hands flat on the chair arms
- OTS Victoria: Lena entering, hair down, coat over her arm
- CU: the silver tea service, untouched

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
Thank you for coming to the house. I asked you here instead of downtown because what I have to say to you should not be said with a microphone in the room.

LENA
I came to the house because I owed you the chance to say it without one. That is the last thing I will owe you today.

VICTORIA
That's fair.

She gestures to the chair across from her. Lena lays the coat over the back of the chair. Sits. Does not look at the tea.

VICTORIA
I drafted four versions of what I am about to say to you. None of them survived contact with the page. So I am going to talk without a page.

LENA
Go ahead.

## S#2. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria's hands stay on the chair arms for the first sentence. They come off for the second. She lets her own hands settle in her lap by the third — a small surrender she has not let herself perform with another person in the room in twenty years.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Victoria across the low table, the fire behind her on one side
- OTS Victoria: Lena seated, listening, hands clasped over one knee
- CU: Victoria's hands on the chair arms — then in her lap
- ECU: the fire in the grate, the small popping of one log

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
The honest sentence is this. I did not protect Mara because I was deceived. I protected Mara because I suspected her, and I suppressed the suspicion to protect this family.

A beat. Lena does not move.

VICTORIA
The scar at her jaw caught the light at a dinner eight months in. I saw it. I told myself I was tired and put the thought away. The second time I saw it was at the rehearsal in March. I told myself it was a trick of the candles. The third time was last June at the lake. I told myself nothing that time because I had stopped letting myself say it inside my own head. By then I was six lies deep into my own silence. The lies were easier than the conversation I would have had to have with my son. I am telling you the order so you understand that I did not miss it. I declined to see it. There is a difference, and the difference is what I owe you the morning of.

LENA
Why did you decline.

VICTORIA
Because in the same eight months I was negotiating two acquisitions whose terms depended on the engagement standing. Because Ethan had not been steady since you were taken from him, and Mara was steadying him in a way I had not seen another woman do. Because I knew your mother once, and a part of me — a small ugly part — was not entirely sorry to see Helena's daughter replaced by a woman who would be easier for me to manage at the long table.

She does not look away while she says it. She does not soften it.

LENA
Thank you for the last sentence.

VICTORIA
That sentence was the one I drafted four times.

## S#3. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria stands. Walks to the small writing desk against the wall. Opens the long drawer. Brings out an envelope. Cream paper, old, the kind of paper that has been somewhere dry and dark for a long time. The handwriting on the front — *For Lena, in confidence* — in a hand Lena recognizes before she has registered recognizing it. Her mother's hand.

Victoria carries the envelope back across the room. Does not extend it. Sets it on the low table beside the silver tea tray. Steps back. Returns to her chair.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: the envelope on the low table, the handwriting visible
- CU: Lena's eyes on the writing, recognition arriving
- OTS Lena: Victoria's face — the controlled mask thinning at the edges
- INSERT: the wax seal on the back of the envelope, the small Sterling monogram pressed into it, intact

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
That's my mother's writing.

VICTORIA
Your mother brought it to me three months before she died. She asked me to keep it. She asked me not to open it. She gave me one instruction for when it should be put into your hand.

LENA
What was the instruction.

VICTORIA
She said *If a woman ever comes forward claiming a place at the table that has not been hers to claim, give this to my daughter. Not before. Not for any other reason. Use your judgment about when the woman has come forward enough.*

LENA
You waited fourteen months after Mara walked through your front door.

VICTORIA
I waited fourteen months. I am giving it to you eleven days after the reception screen. That is the gap between the day a reasonable woman would have given it to you and the day I did. You can hold the gap against me. You should hold the gap against me. I am giving it to you anyway.

A beat. Lena does not pick the envelope up yet. She looks at it on the table. Then at the fire. Then at Victoria.

LENA
There is a sentence I want to ask you before I open it. You said *I knew your mother once.* Once. Past tense. Did you know her well enough that she would have put your name on a trust she founded the year she became pregnant with me.

A beat. Victoria's eyes do not move from Lena's face. The room holds.

VICTORIA
She did not put my name on it. She told me the name of the person she did put on it. She told me one night at the lake the summer before she died. She made me promise that I would not tell you the name unless you asked me directly, with the envelope already in your hand.

LENA
The envelope is on the table.

VICTORIA
It is not yet in your hand.

A beat. Lena reaches across the low table. Picks up the envelope. Holds it. Does not break the seal.

LENA
The envelope is in my hand. Tell me the name.

## S#4. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria's hands come back to the chair arms. The small surrender of the lap is over. The mask is back on — not because she's hiding, but because she's about to say a thing that requires the mask to carry it.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Victoria in the chair, hands back on the arms
- OTS Victoria: Lena across the table, envelope in her hand
- CU: the wax seal under Lena's thumb, not yet broken
- ECU: Victoria's mouth, the first syllable forming

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
The fifth trustee on the Halcyon Trust is named Julian Voss. He is Mara's biological father. He was not married to her mother. He has not been in either of their lives since Mara was four. Your mother knew him decades before she met your father. They had a relationship that ended. The trust was your mother's apology to him for ending it, and her promise to herself that whatever child he eventually had would be looked after — provided that child stayed *clear of you.*

A long beat. Lena's thumb does not press into the seal. It rests on it.

LENA
The child has not stayed clear of me.

VICTORIA
The child has not stayed clear of you. The child has been weaponized against you by the child's mother. Eileen has been running Halcyon as a third trustee for sixteen years without Julian's knowledge — your mother set the trust up so that any one trustee could move assets without the others. She did not anticipate Eileen.

LENA
She anticipated Mara. She did not anticipate Eileen.

VICTORIA
She anticipated the wrong woman.

A beat. Lena looks down at the envelope. The Sterling monogram pressed into the wax. Her thumb still resting on it.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Lena's thumb on the wax seal, the small monogram visible under the print of her finger, the seal still intact.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had thought, walking in, that I would break the seal in this room. I wanted to make her watch me read whatever was inside. Then I thought about my mother — what she would have wanted the morning I learned a man named Julian Voss had been listed on a piece of paper for me before I was born — and I knew she had not addressed the letter to *us.* She had addressed it to me.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(standing, envelope in her hand)
I am going to read this somewhere else. When I have, you and I are going to have one more conversation. After that, whether we have any further conversations will depend on what is in this envelope.

VICTORIA
Thank you for that.

LENA
I'll see myself out.

She does. Victoria stays in the chair in front of the fire, hands on the arms, looking at the empty space on the table where the envelope used to be. The tea has gone cold. She has not poured a cup. She does not pour one now.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP16

## S#1. Noah's Apartment — Living Room · Dusk

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah's apartment — a converted brick loft above his investigator office, two floors up from the street, low warm lamps, the city going amber outside the long windows. The cream envelope from Victoria's office sits in the center of the coffee table, wax seal unbroken, her mother's monogram pressed into it. Lena is alone in the room. Charcoal cashmere turtleneck, dark trousers, mother's necklace at her throat. She is sitting on the edge of the sofa, hands folded in her lap, not touching the envelope.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room, the envelope centered on the coffee table
- MCU Lena seated, hands in her lap
- ECU her thumb passing across the back of her left hand once, slow
- INSERT — the wax seal, intact, the small Sterling monogram

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The envelope has been on three different tables tonight. Victoria's desk. My car seat. This coffee table. I keep moving it like that's the same as opening it.

She stands. She does not pick the envelope up. She crosses the room toward the balcony doors.

## S#2. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Dusk

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The balcony — a narrow steel terrace looking down on a side street, the city stretched dark behind it. Noah is already out there, jacket off, sleeves rolled to the elbow, both forearms resting flat on the iron railing. He doesn't turn when she steps out. He has been waiting for her to come find him on her own time. Lena pulls the glass door behind her. The street noise softens by half.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the balcony, the two of them at the rail, the city low and dim behind
- MCU Noah's forearms on the iron railing — the weight of him grounding it
- OTS Lena to Noah's profile, his eyes on the middle distance
- ECU her hand resting near his on the rail, not touching

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I want to tell you something I haven't told anyone. It's not about the letter.

NOAH
Take whatever order you want with it. I'm not on a clock.

A beat. She watches the street, not him.

LENA
The night Ethan dragged me off the steps of the engagement reception, I went back to my apartment and I tried to figure out what I was feeling. I expected to feel his loss. I had been planning to marry him in seven months. I had picked the food, the music, the line at the end of my own vows.

NOAH
And.

LENA
And what I felt wasn't him. It was the version of me who had been planning to be happy. She was gone. She had been gone since I woke up in that hospital and a nurse asked me what year it was. I have been afraid of her ever since. Of who I am without her.

Noah does not look away from the middle distance. He gives her the silence for as long as she needs it.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA (CONT'D)
I have been afraid that if I get my life back, I'll find out that woman is still gone and I'm just wearing her clothes. I have been carrying that around for fourteen months and I have not said it out loud until right now.

NOAH
Thank you for saying it to me.

LENA
That's all you're going to say.

NOAH
No. I'm just trying to say the rest of it without being clever about it.

## S#3. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
He turns toward her, finally. Both forearms still on the rail. He doesn't move closer. He just turns his face.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Noah, the city lights catching the slight squint at his eye
- OTS Noah to Lena, her hands now resting on the rail beside his
- ECU Lena's hand near his, not yet touching
- CU Noah, speaking without theater

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
I stopped working for Cross Industries the day I saw Mara's wrist in the crash footage. I called Victoria's chief of staff that afternoon and I told him I was off the retainer. I haven't taken a new case in two weeks. I didn't tell you that part because I didn't want you thinking I was here on a clock. I'm not. I'm here because of you, not for you. There's a difference, and I want you to hear it.

LENA
Say it without hedging.

A beat. He looks at her directly. No deflection.

NOAH
I love you. That's the only line I have.

LENA
That makes two of us.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's eyes on his — not glassy, steady
- ECU Noah's hand, the slight uncurl of his fingers on the rail
- OTS Lena to Noah's mouth, just after the line lands

## S#4. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She reaches first. Her hand goes from the rail to the front of his shirt, the heel of her palm against his sternum. He does not move toward her until she does. His forearm weight comes off the railing slowly. His right hand finds the side of her jaw. His thumb rests against the line of her cheekbone. He waits there for one breath. She closes the distance.

They kiss. Slow. Open mouth. His mouth gives her the second she wants to pull back, and she does not pull back.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Noah → Lena CU
- ECU breath stop — Lena's eyes closing
- ECU lips meeting
- CU Lena's hand on his lapel, fingers closing on the fabric
- MCU pull-back two-shot — both of them at the rail, mouths still close
- Wide of the balcony, the city behind, holding

[VISUAL/ACTION]
They break apart. Not fast. His forehead rests against hers a second longer. His thumb is still against her cheekbone. Her hand is still on his shirt. Neither of them apologizes for any of it.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I'm going to read the letter tonight. I want you in the room when I do. Not in this room. Just in the apartment.

NOAH
I'll be in the apartment.

She nods, once. Her hand drops from his shirt. She steps back. She looks at the envelope through the glass, still on the coffee table, the wax seal intact.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had been afraid of who I would be if I got my life back. I hadn't expected to get someone else with it.

## S#5. Across the Street — Lit Window in the Building Opposite · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Cut hard across the side street. A floor and a half above Noah's balcony, in a corner apartment in the building opposite, a single floor lamp burns. A woman stands at the window — Mara, hair severely back, charcoal blouse, surgical scar at her neck unhidden by the angle. She has been holding a pair of compact binoculars to her eyes. She lowers them slowly. The kiss has already happened.

A man's silhouette is behind her — taller, older, half a step out of the light. We do not see his face. A hand settles on her shoulder. The hand does not squeeze. It just rests there.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the apartment opposite, the single floor lamp glowing
- MCU Mara at the window, binoculars in her hand at her side
- ECU the older hand on her shoulder, signet ring catching the lamplight
- OTS Mara to Noah's balcony in the distance — Lena and Noah still at the rail

[DIALOGUE]
VOICE (low, behind her)
Your sister is ready for her real introduction now.

MARA
She's not my sister.

VOICE
She will be by the time I'm finished with this.

[END HOOK]
Mara raises the binoculars again. Through her glass — ECU Lena turning back into the apartment, the envelope still on the coffee table. The hand on Mara's shoulder lifts. The man steps back into the dark behind her without a sound.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**SHE SAW THEM KISS. SHE HAS PLANS.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 17.**

Hard Cut.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A glass-walled lounge looking down on the atrium. ETHAN CROSS sits on the edge of a low couch, suit jacket folded once over his knee. He stands when she enters. In his right hand a small velvet box. He's been holding it long enough that the velvet has gone warm.

- MCU Ethan rising
- ECU the velvet box in his hand
- Two-shot Lena stopping three steps inside the door
- CU Lena's face — composed, not cold

[DIALOGUE]
ETHAN

I told the desk to send you up if you came. Sit down, Ethan. You don't have to stand for me.

ETHAN
I'd like to. For a minute.

He doesn't open the box, but he lifts it once so she sees it.

ETHAN (CONT'D)
I came to ask you something properly. The way I should have done it the first time. My family has already drafted a statement. We can hold a press call tomorrow morning, re-announce the engagement before noon. The original date in the spring. Nothing else changes.

LENA
Ethan. Put that down.

He sets the box on the side table between them. She sits across from him.

LENA (CONT'D)
I appreciate that you came in person. I really do. But I'm going to tell you what I should have said in the hospital, if I'd been clear enough to say anything at all.

Tell me.

LENA
I'm not yours anymore. I haven't been since the morning you stood in front of the Cross Manor front doors and didn't recognize my voice. I know your mother told the guards to remove me. I know you didn't give that order. But I watched your face while it happened, and you didn't look for me. You looked at her. So that's the moment. Not the engagement party, not the hospital — that morning at the doors. That's when it ended.

I know you have. And I want you to keep going. Not with me. With your life. Do good things with the resources you have. Be careful about who's standing next to you when the cameras come on. That's the only thing I want from you.

Is there someone? Noah, I'm guessing.

LENA
Yes. He's the first person who acted like he understood what he had.

He nods. Once. He picks the velvet box up, slides it back into his jacket pocket.

ETHAN
Then I won't waste your morning. Thank you for seeing me, Lena. I mean that.

Take care of yourself, Ethan.

He leaves first. She watches the door close behind him and stays in the chair another full minute before she stands.

He told me once that he sees the moment people stop pretending. I think that's what I just did with Ethan. I stopped pretending I owed him a longer goodbye than he gave me at the reception.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP19

## S#1. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]

## S#2. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- MCU Victoria in the wingback
- OTS Lena watching

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
Your mother and I were not close, but we kept up. Twice a year, lunch at the Madison. The last lunch we had — you would have been about seventeen — she stayed at the table after the plates were cleared. She wasn't a woman who lingered, so I noticed. VICTORIA
I assumed a stalker. A fixated employee, someone she'd dismissed, an old admirer. Helena was a beautiful woman who said no to a great many men. The list of candidates I drew up for her in my head was not short. I told her I'd watch the perimeter when the time came. I thought I knew what perimeter meant.

LENA
And what did she say back to you.

VICTORIA
She said — and these were her exact words, Lena, I have not changed them in fourteen years — she said: "Victoria. It will be someone with my face."

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's hand finding the necklace
- CU Lena's face — the small involuntary swallow
- ECU Victoria's eyes holding steady

VICTORIA (CONT'D)
I asked her if she meant a relative. She said it wasn't the kind of relative she could explain to me over coffee. I let it sit. She paid for lunch. We never had that conversation again. She was diagnosed eleven months later and gone in another fourteen.

LENA
So when Ethan brought me home, you remembered that lunch.

VICTORIA
I remembered every word of it. I hired the firm three days after he told me he was going to propose. I told myself I was protecting the family from a scandal. I was telling myself a more comfortable version of what I was actually afraid of.

LENA
Which was.

VICTORIA
That your mother had been right, and that I had let fourteen years go by without doing one useful thing about it.

## S#3. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena does not stand. She sets her hand flat on the arm of the chair and leaves it there. Victoria, for the first time in the conversation, looks tired.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot from a low angle, the fireplace dark between them
- ECU Lena's hand on the chair arm, knuckles paled by pressure
- CU Victoria's face — the corner of her mouth pulling in once

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Did you ever look into who that person might be. In all those years. Did you ever once put a name to her warning.

VICTORIA
I'm going to give you the answer you already know. I'm sorry, Lena. I let it slide. After she died I told myself she'd been sick longer than the diagnosis. The lunch had been the first sign, and I made her the unreliable narrator of her own life so I wouldn't have to do the work she asked me to do. That is the truth, and I am ashamed of it, and I'm telling you in this room because I owe you the conversation I did not give her.

LENA
Mara walked into my engagement and you stood in front of her at the Cross Manor front doors and told the guards to remove me.

VICTORIA
I did.

LENA
You looked at her face, which is my face, and you did not stop.

VICTORIA
I did not stop. I have walked back through that morning every night since the reception, and I want you to know that. I'm not asking you to forgive it — I'm telling you I sat with it.

LENA
Did your husband know about the lunch. About what my mother said.

VICTORIA
Charles was abroad that month. I never brought it up afterward. It was the kind of thing one filed away. I filed it away.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Mom warned a woman who didn't want to be warned. I'm sitting in front of that woman now, and I can see for the first time that she's not the villain of this. She's the witness who looked the other way. Those are not the same thing. They are also not very different.

## S#4. Cross Manor — Drive · Late Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena outside in the gravel drive. Her car waiting. NOAH KEENE leans against the passenger door, arms crossed, sunglasses pushed up into his hair. He pushes off as she approaches.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the manor behind her
- MCU two-shot at the car
- ECU Lena's hand opening the door, then closing it, then leaning her forearm on the roof
- CU her face — composed but the eyes wet at the corners

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
That long inside, huh.

LENA
She told me what my mother had told her at a lunch I wasn't at, fourteen years ago. My mother had said someone was going to come for me — someone with her face. Victoria thought it meant a stalker. It didn't.

NOAH
So she knew the whole time.

LENA
She knew enough to be warned. She didn't know enough to act. That's a different kind of failure than the one I came here to accuse her of, and I don't know what to do with it yet.

NOAH
You don't have to do anything with it today. You sit with it. We keep working.

LENA
The man in the lobby photo.

NOAH
I have a name. Probable. I want one more confirmation before I bring it to you. Get in the car. I'll drive.

She nods. Gets in. The manor's front door, behind them, stays open. Victoria stands in the doorway. She doesn't lift a hand.

[INTERCUT]

- Victoria still in the doorway, watching the car go, then turning back into the dark of the hall

[END HOOK]
INSERT — Lena's phone on the dashboard. A new message, unknown sender.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
UNKNOWN: She told you about the lunch. She didn't tell you what your mother did the year before.

Lena reads it. Sets the phone face-down. Looks straight ahead through the windshield. Noah, driving, glances over.

NOAH (CONT'D)
Same number.

LENA
Then drive faster.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP20

## S#1. Halcyon Trust — Filing Depot · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A sub-level storage floor of a private trust company — fluorescent strips, rolling shelves on tracks, the air dry and cold enough to see breath. NOAH KEENE in dark wool coat over a black sweater, ID lanyard clipped at his hip — a borrowed one, not his — moves through the stacks with a small flashlight tucked into his collar. A heavyset RECORDS CLERK in his late fifties shadows him, two steps behind, holding a clipboard.

Understood. Pull row forty-one. Year of incorporation marked 1995. Settlor surname beginning S.

You're looking at one box.

NOAH
I'm looking at one box.

The clerk cranks the wheel on row forty-one. The shelf opens. He runs a finger along the labels, stops on a brown banker's box, slides it forward, sets it on a metal table in the aisle.

## S#2. Halcyon Trust — Filing Depot · Late Night (continuous)

- ECU the first page — original incorporation papers, typewritten 1995
- CU Noah's eyes moving down the page, line by line

[GRAPHIC/UI]

- DATE OF EXECUTION: 14 March 1995
- BENEFICIARY OF RECORD: [REDACTED — black bar]
- BENEFICIARY DOB: 17 December 1995 (in blue ink, hand-corrected once)
- TRUSTEE: HALCYON TRUST COMPANY

[VO]
NOAH (V.O.)
Lena was born March of 1996. Three months earlier than that — December of 1995 — there was already a beneficiary waiting in a trust her mother had set up. Her mother executed this document seven months pregnant with Lena. She knew exactly what she was doing.

[DIALOGUE]
CLERK
You found what you came for.

He photographs each page in order, flashlight angled to kill the glare on the laminated sleeve. The clerk turns his back at the right moment and pretends to be checking the row number.

[INTERCUT]

- ECU her fingertips lifting the paperweight off. Setting it aside.
- ECU her thumb resting on the wax seal — not breaking it. Just touching.
- CU her face — she has been sitting like this since Noah left.

A hospital room, fourteen months ago. Lena, hair still long, eyes half-lidded under sedation. A nurse leaning over with a chart. From Lena's blurred POV — a second figure at the foot of the bed, a woman with her own face, smiling once and turning away before the focus clears. The image dissolves.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I told the doctors I'd hallucinated her. They wrote it down as a sedation artifact. Tonight I'm not so sure anymore.

## S#3. Noah's Car — Parking Garage · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah's car in the deserted garage. Engine off. Dome light on. The folder of photographs spread across the passenger seat. He calls Lena on speaker.

- ECU the phone screen — call connected, LENA
- OTS the photographs on the seat

[INTERCUT]

Three months before me.

Three months before you. Your mother set up an irrevocable trust seven months into her pregnancy with you, naming another beneficiary born three months earlier. That's not a contingency plan, Lena. That's an existing child.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's apartment, low lamp light. The photographs spread on the coffee table. Lena sits on the rug in front of the couch, knees drawn up under her, mother's necklace warm against the hollow of her throat. NOAH sits on the couch behind her, one hand on the back of her neck, not moving — just resting.

[KEY CAMERA]

- MCU Lena from the front, the photos in her lap
- CU Noah's hand at her nape, thumb still
- ECU the date in blue ink on the corrected document

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
She set this up when I was a heartbeat inside her — time to think about the wording, choose a trustee, redact the name before any of us could see it. That wasn't a panic. That was a plan.

NOAH
It was a plan.

LENA
A plan to provide for someone she could not name out loud to my father. A plan that she expected to outlive her, since she made it irrevocable. A plan she did not tell me about even when she was dying.

NOAH
You think she meant to.

LENA
The letter. The one on the desk. I think she meant to.

She turns her head once toward the desk. Doesn't get up. Noah's hand at her nape settles a degree warmer.

NOAH
You don't have to open it tonight. You don't have to open it tomorrow either. We have a date of birth and a redacted name. That's enough work for tonight.

LENA
The man in the lobby photo.

NOAH
I'm running the cheekbone against the trust company's known associates and against your mother's old social register. I'll have a probable name by morning.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Mom set up a trust three months before I was born. She named a child who already existed. She paid a company called Halcyon to hold the file for thirty years and counting. I am furious with her for not telling me, and I miss her so much it crowds my chest. Both of those are true. I do not know how to put them down.

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- CU Lena noticing

[DIALOGUE]

Noah.

Since I came up. He's not hiding. He wants you to see him.

NOAH
Same plates. Or the same lack of them.

He doesn't move his hand from her nape. She doesn't move her head from his knee. The curb car sits.

LENA (CONT'D)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena on her couch, phone on the coffee table on speaker. The photograph and the typewritten note bagged in a Ziploc beside it. ANDREA QUINN — Lena's family PR head, late forties, dressed for a hospital visit she's running between — answers on the second ring.

Mara Voss. Out loud. To the cameras.

Five-thirty. Thank you, Andrea. Go in.

Mom left me a letter and a question and a girl with my face. I'm going to answer all three of them in the order I choose. Mara wants a private negotiation. She is going to get the most public conversation of either of our lives.

## S#5. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Afternoon (continuous)

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP22

## S#1. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A rooftop terrace forty stories up — a clear morning, the city laid flat behind glass partitions. Twelve chairs in three rows of four, a low podium with a single microphone, the Sterling family monogram on a discreet plaque. A small bank of press: a Times reporter, a Journal reporter, an AP correspondent, three independents, two staffers from the trade press, three more cleared seats. LENA STERLING in the wing, charcoal pencil skirt and navy silk blouse, mother's necklace at her throat, no other jewelry. ANDREA QUINN at her shoulder with a single index card.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the rooftop, podium centered, press seated
- MCU Lena in the wing
- ECU her thumb on the index card
- OTS Andrea murmuring close to her ear

[DIALOGUE]
ANDREA
The legal team wants you to keep her surname out of it. I'm telling them you won't. I'm telling them you'll name her three times in two minutes and they should make their peace with it before the camera turns on.

LENA
Tell them I'll name her four times.

ANDREA
You will not.

LENA
Three. I'll keep it at three.

ANDREA
Take a breath. Go when the red light is on.

The red light on the camera in front of the podium flicks on.

## S#2. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena walks to the podium. No prepared sheet on the surface — only the index card she does not put down. She rests one hand lightly on the wood, the other at her side.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena at the podium
- OTS over the press row, the city behind
- CU her face, calm, the necklace catching morning light
- Intercut occasional reaction shots — Times reporter writing, AP correspondent's recorder light

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Good morning. Thank you for coming on short notice. I'll be brief and I'll take questions for ten minutes after.

She pauses. Lets the room settle.

LENA (CONT'D)
Most of you covered my engagement and the reception last spring. Most of you covered the correction of the public record at the Cross family announcement in October. I'm not here today to revisit either of those events. I'm here because of my mother.

ECU — her thumb finds the edge of the necklace once, returns to the podium.

LENA (CONT'D)
Helena Sterling died eight years ago next March. Before she died, she wrote me a letter. I have not opened it. It has been in my family's papers since the week after her funeral. I will tell you, plainly, why I have not opened it — because I assumed for eight years that whatever it contained was meant to bring me peace, and I was not in a hurry for that kind of peace. I am opening it this week, on my own terms. I'm telling you about it now because I've been given a reason to.

She lifts the index card once. Sets it back down without reading from it.

LENA (CONT'D)
For the last several weeks, a woman who is not me has been representing herself as me to private actors in this city and abroad. Some of you covered the original incident at the reception when she did the same in public. Her name is Mara Voss. She is the same woman who stood on that stage last spring under my legal name. She has, in the last forty-eight hours, contacted me personally in writing, with a financial demand, attached to a claim that my mother's private papers contain information she intends to monetize.

A small wave through the press row. Three pens moving faster. The Times reporter looks up.

LENA (CONT'D)
I'm here to say two things to her in front of all of you, since she has been clear she prefers the leverage of privacy. First — I will not be paying her. Not today, not next week, not in any month. Second — there is a question about my family's history that I intend to answer, and I intend to answer it in my mother's voice, not in hers. I am opening the letter this week. Whatever it says, I will share what is appropriate to share. There is a woman who believes she has a right to my family name. We will deal with that in due course, in public, and on my schedule, not hers.

She lets the moment hold.

LENA (CONT'D)
I will take questions.

## S#3. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Hands go up in the press row. The Times reporter first.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU the Times reporter
- OTS Lena fielding
- ECU the AP recorder's red light

[DIALOGUE]
TIMES REPORTER
Ms. Sterling — are you alleging Mara Voss is the woman who appeared at the Cross-Sterling engagement last spring under your name. Is this the same person.

LENA
Yes. That is what I'm alleging, on the public record, with this microphone in front of me. The Cross family has been informed of that allegation through their counsel as of yesterday afternoon. I have no further comment on Cross family matters from this podium.

JOURNAL REPORTER
You said she contacted you with a financial demand. Are you in possession of evidence of that contact.

LENA
I am. It was hand-delivered yesterday morning. It is in the custody of my private security and has been logged with my legal team. I will not be reading it aloud today. I will note that it includes a photograph from my mother's personal collection, which raises its own questions about access to my family's records. Those questions will be answered separately.

AP CORRESPONDENT
You said you'd be opening your mother's letter this week. Are you planning a follow-on statement after you do.

LENA
That depends on what's in it. If it's a private letter, it remains private. If it contains information that bears on Ms. Voss's claims, I will return to this podium. I will not be ambushed into a tabloid timeline. If a leak appears before I'm ready, you will know it was not mine.

INDEPENDENT REPORTER
Is Noah Keene here today.

LENA
He is not in this room. He's not part of this conversation. He's part of my private life, which is something Ms. Voss has also tried to weaponize this week. I'd ask you not to do the same favor for her. Next question.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
She wanted me to text a number — she got a podium instead. She wanted my mother's letter as her leverage; I just made it my schedule. That is the work for today.

## S#4. Mara's Safe Apartment — Living Room · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Cut hard to MARA VOSS in a furnished short-term rental, the press conference live on a wall-mounted screen. She stands. Pacing. Hair severely back, charcoal blouse, surgical scar on her neck unhidden. Lena's face fills the screen behind her.

- ECU her hand on her phone, knuckles white

[DIALOGUE]
LENA (ON SCREEN)
…we will deal with that in due course, in public, and on my schedule, not hers.

Mara stops. Stares at the screen.

MARA
She named me.

She brings the phone up. Dials. A man's voice answers — clipped, calm, older. We do not see him.

VOICE (V.O.)
You're watching it.

MARA
She moved faster than we wanted. Are we ready or not.

VOICE (V.O.)
Then move with her. We're more than ready. The girl thinks she's accelerating — she's not. She's stepping onto the schedule we already wrote. Go tonight. Knock at the door yourself. Let her think she's chosen the room.

Mara breathes out. Sets the phone on the counter. Looks at the screen again — Lena stepping down from the podium, the press rising.

MARA
And the letter.

VOICE (V.O.)
The letter is the next move. Not yours. Not mine. Hers. Be in the room when she opens it.

He hangs up. Mara picks up her coat.

## S#5. Sterling Holdings — Backstage Hallway · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena steps off the podium and into the hallway. ANDREA falls in beside her. NOAH KEENE waits at the end of the corridor, leaning a shoulder against the wall. He doesn't smile. He nods, once, slow.

- ECU his hand brushing her elbow once, releasing

ANDREA
The Times is going wide at noon. The Journal will run a sidebar on the financial threat by three. AP has the wire moving now. The trades are already writing. Twitter is on fire and I will not be reading it. Are you all right.

LENA
I'm fine. I'd like a glass of water that isn't sparkling.

ANDREA
There's an entire pitcher in the green room. Drink it.

Andrea peels off to handle the press exit. Lena and Noah are alone in the hallway.

NOAH
You said her name three times.

Andrea would have killed me if you'd done four.

LENA
She told me she'd kill me. I made my peace with three.

He looks at her — direct, warm, no theater.

She'll move tonight. He told her to.

Helena. You raised a daughter who fights forward. I'll give you that. But she's about to walk into a room she didn't set up. Let's see what your letter says when she finally reads it.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP23

## S#1. Lena's Apartment — Entry Hall · Early Evening

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A high-floor apartment in a quiet building, the city going on without them outside the long windows. The press conference was this morning; the last three days have been phone calls and lawyers. Lena is in her socks at the entry hall when the lock turns. NOAH lets himself in — one paper grocery bag in each arm, a leather camera bag still over his shoulder. No flowers. Bread. Lemons. A cut of fish in butcher paper at the top of one bag. He sets the bags on the console table. He hangs the camera bag on the hook. He looks at her.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the entry hall, the bags between them
- MCU Noah setting the bags down, sleeves already pushed up
- ECU his hand setting a single lemon on the console
- OTS Lena watching him

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
I'm making dinner. You're not going to argue with me about it.

LENA
I wasn't going to.

NOAH
Good. Then we don't have to have that conversation.

He picks the bags back up, walks past her toward the kitchen. She follows without thinking about it.

## S#2. Lena's Apartment — Kitchen · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The kitchen — open plan, stone counter, a pendant lamp over the island. Noah unloads the bags methodically. He doesn't ask her where things go; he opens drawers until he finds them. He puts a pan on the heat. He starts a small pot of water for greens. He moves like someone who has cooked in other people's kitchens for a long time. Lena leans her hip against the island, arms folded loose. She watches him.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Noah at the counter, knife in hand, lemon halved
- ECU his forearms, the tendons working as he cuts
- OTS Lena leaning on the island
- CU Lena's face — softer than it has looked in three days

[INTERCUT]
COUNTER: Noah's hands working, deliberate.
LENA: watching him with no particular expression, the line of her shoulders dropping by half.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I haven't told you about the third page of the letter. I told you about the first two. There's a third page. It's still sealed inside the second one. She did it in three layers.

NOAH
I figured.

LENA
You figured.

NOAH
You've been reaching toward your throat every time the letter comes up. The second page wouldn't make you do that. The first page wouldn't either. You're worried about something you haven't read yet.

He doesn't look up from the knife. He says it the way he might tell her there's no garlic.

LENA
I think she knew something was wrong when she wrote it. The handwriting goes uneven by the third page. The first two are her normal hand. The third page — even sealed — I can see the edge of the writing where the wax broke around it. It's not the same hand.

NOAH
Cancer. That's what she had at the end.

LENA
She had eight more weeks at that point. She was still walking. She was still answering the phone. But the third page — I think the third page is the part she didn't want to write while she could still be talked out of it.

NOAH
And you don't want to read it alone.

LENA
I don't want to read it alone.

He sets the knife down. He turns the heat under the pan to low. He wipes his hands on a dish towel.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Noah's hands on the dish towel, deliberate
- MCU Noah turning to face her
- OTS Noah to Lena's face

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Then we don't read it tonight. We read it when you say. In this apartment. I'll be in the room. I'll read it with you if you want me to, or I'll sit at the counter while you read it to me, or I'll be in the other room — your call. Not tonight. When you're ready.

LENA
You're sure?

NOAH
I am. Trust me on this one.

LENA
You haven't even asked me what I think is in it.

NOAH
That's not the question I'm working on tonight. Tonight's question is whether you've eaten anything since this morning. And the answer to that is no.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
He never asks the question he's already answered for himself. It is one of the things about him I have not gotten used to.

## S#3. Lena's Apartment — Kitchen · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She doesn't decide to do it. Her hand goes onto his arm without her permission. Just above the elbow. Her palm against his forearm — the muscle, the heat of his skin, the rolled cuff of his shirt. He stops moving. He looks down at her hand. Then up at her face.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's hand on his forearm, the cuff just above her fingers
- ECU Noah's eyes locking on hers
- OTS Lena → Noah, two-shot at the island

[VISUAL/ACTION]
He sets the dish towel down on the stone counter without looking at it. He turns toward her, slow. His right hand comes up. He puts it at the side of her jaw, the way he put it at the side of her jaw on the balcony seven weeks ago. He waits a second longer than he needs to. He is asking, and she is the one answering.

She closes the distance.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Lena → Noah
- ECU eyes locked, the second of his patience
- CU his hand on her jaw — not her thigh, not her ribs, her jaw
- ECU kiss — lips meeting, slow, open mouth
- MCU sit-down hold, her back against the counter, his forearm braced beside her on the stone

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The kiss does not escalate. He holds her there at the counter, one forearm flat on the stone beside her, the other hand still at her jaw. She is the one who pulls back first — not fast, not far. Her forehead rests against his collarbone. His hand stays at her jaw. He does not move it.

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
The pan's going to scorch.

LENA
Move the pan.

He reaches blindly behind himself, slides the pan off the heat with the back of his wrist. He does not let go of her jaw to do it. She laughs, once — small, real, the first time she has laughed in three days.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Living Room Floor · Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
They are sitting on the floor with their backs against the sofa, plates in their laps, the lamp low. Two glasses of water on the rug between them. The food is on the plates, not on the table. Neither of them has commented on it. Outside the long windows, the city has gone fully dark.

- MCU side-on, plates in their laps
- ECU Lena's hand reaching across to take a piece of bread off his plate
- Two-shot, both of them eating slow

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You can't cook like that and live alone.

NOAH
I do though — and I know that's not what you said.

A beat. She takes another piece of bread. Doesn't ask permission.

LENA
Were you a cook before you were an investigator.

NOAH
I was a Marine before I was a cook. The cooking is from the Marine part, actually.

LENA
I'm not going to ask about the Marine part tonight.

NOAH
I wasn't going to volunteer it.

They eat in quiet for a long beat. Comfortable. She leans her shoulder against his shoulder. He does not adjust to take her weight; he just lets it land.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had forgotten that you are allowed to eat dinner on the floor.

MARA (ON SCREEN)
Can we talk in person finally. I'm not here to hurt anyone tonight. I'm at your door in about a minute. You can answer it or you can call your security, but if you call security you will not get the conversation I came to give you. I came alone. I'll be at your door in about a minute.

The feed cuts. The phone goes black.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP24

## S#1. Lena's Apartment — Entry Hall · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The faint hum of the elevator settles. A pause in the hallway outside. Lena is already on her feet, crossing to the door. Noah a half-step behind her, not touching her — just there. She does not look through the peephole. She undoes the deadbolt herself. She opens the door before Mara has the chance to knock.

Mara is in the corridor. Hands at her sides, no purse, charcoal blouse the way she wore it on Lena's lobby camera ninety seconds ago. The surgical scar two centimeters under her left ear is not covered tonight. She has not done her hair to hide it. She looks at Lena. She looks past Lena at Noah. She looks back at Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]

- OTS Lena to Mara in the corridor

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
Thank you for opening it.

Come in. Take off your coat. Sit on the couch, not the chair. I want to see your hands.

That's fair.

She steps inside. She takes off her coat and hangs it herself on the hook beside Noah's camera bag. She walks past them into the living room. She sits on the couch where Lena told her to sit. She puts her hands flat on her knees, palms down. She is not performing it. It is the posture of someone who has been waiting a long time to do exactly this.

[INTERCUT]
- The building's lobby, twenty floors below. An older man on the long bench by the security desk — dark coat, dark trousers, a leather satchel at his side. He has been searched. The two night guards are watching him from the desk. He has not looked at the elevator bank once. His hands are folded in his lap.
- ECU his hand, the knuckles white where one thumb has been pressing the other for some time.

## S#2. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The plates from earlier are still on the rug. Noah collects them in one quiet motion and carries them to the kitchen. He does not leave the apartment. He stays at the kitchen island, twenty feet away, where he can see and hear. Lena sits in the armchair across from Mara. She crosses her ankles. She waits.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room — Mara on the couch, Lena in the chair, Noah at the island in the background
- MCU Mara, palms flat on her knees
- OTS Mara to Lena, the necklace at Lena's throat catching the lamp
- ECU Mara's eyes — tired, not soft

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
I'm not going to apologize tonight. I want to say that before I start, because I have been told a hundred times in the last three weeks that I should walk in here and grovel. I'm not going to. I don't think you'd believe me if I did, and you would be right not to. I'm here to give you a version of the story you have not heard.

LENA
Go ahead.

MARA
My mother told me from the time I was a kid that I was Helena Sterling's hidden daughter. I'm going to call her my mother in this room, because that's what she has been to me my whole life, and I'm not ready to call her by her name in front of you. She showed me documents at twenty-four. She gave me my real name. She said you had what was supposed to be mine. The man who raised me was not my father. The woman whose name I'd been using on every form was not the woman who gave birth to me. I believed her. I want you to understand I believed her then.

LENA
Go on.

MARA
I went to my mother's side of the story first. I think most people would have. The story she gave me was that Helena Sterling had an affair, she had a child, and she let that child be raised in another house because the affair would have ended her marriage and her access to your father's company. The story I was given was that I had been hidden so that you could exist as the only daughter. Whether any of that is true is a question I am no longer sure of the answer to. I believed it then. I want you to understand that I believed it then.

LENA
Keep going.

MARA
I thought I was taking back my own family name. I thought you would be told the truth and I thought you and I would have to figure out what to do with the family on the other side of it. I did not know I was going to be made into a face. I did not know about Dr. Brandt until I was already in the chair. I thought I had signed up for a smaller procedure — work on my jaw, work on my cheek. I woke up nine hours later. The first time I saw myself in the mirror after the surgery I threw up.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara on the couch, not crying — flat, used-up
- ECU Lena's hands in her lap, fingers loose
- OTS Lena to Mara, Noah in the deep background at the island
- CU Mara, the scar at her ear caught at this angle

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (CONT'D)
By the time I understood what I had been turned into, the version of me that had agreed to go this far was someone I could not get back to. The crash on the cliff road happened two weeks after I came out of bandages. I did not drive that car. I did not arrange it either, but I'll tell you who did — not in this sentence, and not without help in this room.

## S#3. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena does not interrupt. She has not interrupted once. She lets Mara finish. She lets the silence sit. When she finally speaks, she speaks low and even.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena, the lamp catching half her face
- ECU her hand, finally lifting from her lap, settling on the arm of the chair
- OTS Lena to Mara on the couch

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
If everything you just said is true, then you have been used. The person who used you is still using you. I'm not interested in arguing the parts of what you said that I'm not sure I believe. We can get to those later. I want one piece of information from you tonight, and I want it before we go any further. Who told you about Helena being your mother. By name. Not "my mother." Not "the woman who raised me." The name.

A long beat. Mara's hands stay flat on her knees. She looks past Lena at Noah. She looks back at Lena.

MARA
I'll give you the name tonight, but I want one other person in the room when I do. He's downstairs — has been for an hour. He came to your building before I did. If you let me, I'll bring him up.

LENA
Who is he.

MARA
The person who can confirm everything I just said. And a few things I haven't.

Lena looks at Noah. Noah looks at her. He nods once, slow. He lifts his phone — already on the lobby camera feed.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Noah's phone, the lobby feed live — an older man visible alone on a bench
- MCU Lena's face deciding
- OTS Lena to Mara

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Tell building security he can come up. Tell them I'm expecting him. Tell them I want the elevator security camera live on Noah's phone the whole way.

MARA

I'll make my own decision about that when he's in this room.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Entry Hall · A Few Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The doorbell. Mara stands. She walks past Lena, asks her quietly with her eyes if she can. Lena nods, once. Mara opens the door herself.

JULIAN VOSS — mid-sixties, gaunt, the kind of thin that comes from travel and from worry. Dark coat, dark trousers, no tie, a leather satchel at his side that has been opened by security tonight. Hair white at the temples and combed back. He carries himself like a man who used to be in better rooms than this. He steps inside. He does not move past the threshold without permission.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
Come in, Julian.

She steps back. She turns to Lena and Noah. The introduction comes out flat and unrehearsed.

MARA (CONT'D)
This is Julian Voss. My real father. He has not seen me in person in nineteen years. He has been in hiding for fourteen of them. The voice you heard on the phone in my apartment after your press conference yesterday morning — that was not him. He has been the one trying to pull me out of the room she's been keeping me in. Julian, this is Lena. This is Noah.

Julian steps fully into the apartment. He sets the satchel down by the door. He looks at Lena. His hands are at his sides. He does not extend one to her. He has the manners not to ask.

[DIALOGUE]
JULIAN
(quietly)
Ms. Sterling. I am sorry for what your mother did not have a chance to tell you.

LENA
You knew my mother.

JULIAN
I knew her for thirty-one years. I knew her well enough that she would not have written that letter in three sealed sections if she had not been planning for the night you and I were standing in the same room. She knew that I would be in a room with you eventually. She did not know it would take this long.

No.

It was a man who was your mother's friend for fifteen years and her enemy for the last seven. He is the third trustee on a fund called Halcyon. You found the name of that fund last fall in a folder in the bridal room. His name is on the third signature line on a document I am going to give you tonight. He has been using my daughter as a face for that fund's purposes since the day she came out of bandages. I have been trying to get her out of his reach since the week after the crash. I have not been successful. I am here tonight because I am out of moves I can make alone.

And you believe you can prove all of that.

I have brought what I could carry through three airports. The rest is in a safety deposit box in Lisbon under a name your mother gave me twenty-six years ago. I will give you the name of the box and the name on it before I leave this apartment tonight.

## S#1. Sterling Holdings — Press Wing · Midday · Two Days Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A clean, low-ceilinged press wing at the back of Sterling Holdings. Not an atrium. Not a podium. Just three long folding tables along one wall, folders stacked at each — beige clasp envelopes, plain labels, no Sterling crest. LENA is not in this room.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Andrea told me to leave the building before the packets went out. She said I shouldn't be in the same postal code as the cameras today. I stayed anyway. I went up forty floors instead of out. I wanted to see if I would feel anything when the doors opened on the women who had spent a year wearing pieces of my life.

The reporters open the folders. Inside: a single page wire-transfer ledger with TESSA HALE highlighted as the recipient of three payments from a numbered account; a clinic file print-out with CELIA BRANDT, MD on the surgeon line and the same MARA EILEEN VOSS post-op heading the room knows from the engagement-night feed; a copy of a private gambling-debt buyout dated six weeks before the cliff-road crash, also routed through the same numbered account. TESSA HALE — late-20s, auburn waves, camel cardigan over a cream camisole, gold hoops — walks toward the small bank of photographers at the east exit. She has not been arrested. She steps into the foyer. The photographers see the box. They start. The flashbulbs come fast. She does not put a hand up. She keeps walking. She has been told by Andrea Quinn personally that putting a hand up will be the photograph that runs.

Tessa is past the photographers. She is at the curb. A black town car is waiting — not for her. She does not get into it. She walks. The cameras follow her for fourteen steps before they lose interest. She turns a corner.

Did Andrea call.

NOAH
The Times will run tomorrow. The Journal already filed. The medical board issued a single paragraph at two. The trades will spend a week on it. There is no formal proceeding from any of this. It will be informal industry consequence — quiet calls, dropped accounts, no contracts at renewal. Andrea says that's the version that lasts.

She turns from the window. She picks up the coffee. She looks at him.

LENA
I want to read the third page tonight.

NOAH
I'll be here.

## S#5. Lena's Office — Desk · After Dark

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The building has gone mostly quiet. Lena sits behind her desk. Lamp on, overhead off. The letter is in front of her — her mother's cream paper, the first two pages unfolded to the right of the desk pad, both faces-down so the writing does not look up at her. The third page is still sealed. The wax over the third section is darker than the wax on the front of the envelope, a different batch. Helena sealed it later, possibly alone. Noah is at the small couch by the door, jacket off, sleeves rolled, reading nothing, just being in the room.

Lena picks up the slim letter knife from the desk caddy. She slides it under the wax of the third seal. The seal lifts cleanly. She unfolds the page.

She does not read it aloud.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the wax seal lifting under the knife
- ECU the page unfolding — handwriting visible but unreadable in this shot
- MCU Lena's face — the lamp catching her
- HOLD on her face. Camera does not pan to the page.
- ECU her eyes moving line by line. One. Two. Three. Then back up to the top of the page.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Her expression changes once. A small tightening at the mouth. She reads on. It changes a second time — a slow pull at the eyebrow, recognition gathering. She turns the page. She reads the back. It changes a third time. Her hand, which has been holding the page lightly, sets the page flat on the desk and does not move. The final expression is not grief. It is not surprise. It is cold realization. Of something specific. Of something that has now made the rest of the letter make a kind of sense.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's eyes, very still
- ECU her hand flat on the page, fingers spread, no tremor
- MCU pull back — Lena alone behind the desk, Noah on the couch in the background

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother wrote the third page in October — after the diagnosis, before the radiation. She wrote it for the night a particular person walked into a particular room. For eight years, I'd assumed that person was me.

She turns the page over once more. There is one final line at the bottom of the second side, in the unsteady hand of the last two weeks before her mother's death. Lena's eyes stop on it.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU one line in Helena's failing hand, visible to camera for the first time: *Forgive me for the daughter I let her keep.*

She does not read the line aloud.

## S#6. Lena's Office — Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The intercom on the desk buzzes once. Then again. Lena does not jump. She has been expecting it for two hours.

- MCU Lena looking up from the page, slow
- OTS Noah from the couch, already standing
- INSERT — the building's lobby camera on Lena's monitor: Mara at the desk, in the same charcoal blouse as two nights ago, alone.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena reaches for the intercom. She does not press the button yet. She looks at Noah. Noah looks back at her — not stopping her, not pushing her. Just present. Lena's other hand is still flat on the third page of the letter.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (ON INTERCOM)
I told you you'd want me in the room when you finally got to the third page. I'm in the lobby. I'm alone. Julian's not with me tonight. Open the door.

A beat. Lena's eyes go to the line at the bottom of the page that her mother wrote in the unsteady hand of the last two weeks. She does not say what it says.

[KEY CAMERA]

- ECU the line on the page — held just out of focus, unreadable
- MCU Lena, deciding
- ECU her thumb pressing the button down

[END HOOK]
[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into the intercom, quiet, level)

She lifts her thumb. The lobby line clicks closed. Noah crosses to the desk. He stops on the opposite side of it. He does not ask her what was on the third page. He waits.

- ECU the wax of the third seal lying broken on the desk pad

**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 26.**

## S#1. The Envelope · Lena's apartment · early morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena at the kitchen island. The letter from EP25 is open on the marble in front of her. Her mother's handwriting, slanted, faded. Three pages, one photograph paper-clipped to the back. Noah stands at the window, arms crossed, watching her read it again. The kettle clicks off behind him. He doesn't pour.

The letter doesn't finish that sentence. That's the part she didn't write down.

(reading, voice flatter)

Meaning my mother had a reason to want him stayed quiet that didn't have anything to do with a child.

Lena folds the letter back into thirds. Slides it into the inside pocket of her coat. Looks at Noah. "Mara needs to think we still don't know."


## S#2. Outside the City · small brick house · late morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A modest street on the edge of the city. Row houses. A small garden gate. Mara, in a plain black coat, stands at the gate of one house. She doesn't go in. She walks past it, two blocks, to a small cemetery behind a chapel. Headstones, no statues. She stops at one. Carved name: EILEEN MARGAUX VOSS. Below it, a date — twenty-six years ago.

- ECU her hand resting flat on top of the headstone
- High angle pulling back — Mara small in the empty plot

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(quiet, to the stone)
I read it. The letter. Lena has it now — she's going to put it together piece by piece, because that's what she does.

MARA

MARA
Say something.

A man's gloved hand settles on Mara's shoulder from behind. We see only the glove — black leather, well-worn. Mara doesn't flinch. She lowers her head further.

Father. She opened the letter.

NOAH
What do you want to do.

LENA
Ethan's going to defend me publicly inside the hour. Watch.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
A second news clip on Lena's monitor — Ethan Cross at the door of Cross Industries, microphones around him. Audio:
ETHAN (on screen): "Lena Sterling is who she has always been. The petition is unfounded. We support her completely."

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
He's clean about it.

LENA
He's finally clean about it. Took him long enough.


## S#3. The private text · noon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's phone vibrates again. She turns it over. It's Mara. A long message. She reads it once, then sets the phone down. Reads it again.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's phone screen, message thread visible
- CU her thumb hovering over the screen
- MCU Lena's face, controlled

[GRAPHIC/UI]
Text from MARA visible on screen:
"Whatever you find about me, I have to finish this. I started it and I don't know how to stop. If you have something to say to me, say it to my face. Not through a press release. — M."

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Don't answer that here.

LENA
I'm answering it.

NOAH
Where.

LENA
The lake house. Tomorrow. Just her and me, no lawyers. If somebody's running her, they won't let her go alone. I want to see who shows up at the gate.

NOAH
I'll be on the ridge. Two hundred yards out.

LENA
I know you will.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena types the reply. We see it on screen:

She sends. Slides the phone away.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara, in soft light, reading Lena's reply. She nods once to herself. Behind her — out of focus — a figure in a dark coat sits on the couch, only the gloved hand visible from EP26 again, this time resting on the armrest. A glass of water in the other hand. Voice off-screen, careful, female-toned but lowered into a register that isn't quite Mara's mother's voice as we'll know it later — not yet.

- MCU Mara from over the figure's shoulder, slightly obscured
- ECU the gloved hand on the armrest
- CU Mara's mouth, controlled

[DIALOGUE]
FIGURE (O.S.)
Don't go. She's setting you up.

MARA
I don't think so.

You don't think a lot of things now. Two months ago you were certain.

Two months ago I was running someone else's script. I want to hear hers in her own voice.

FIGURE (O.S.)
And what if she tells you what we don't want her to tell you.

MARA
Then I'll know what we are.

[END HOOK]
Mara pockets her phone. Doesn't turn to look at the figure. She walks to the door, lifts her coat off the hook, and leaves the apartment. The gloved hand stays on the armrest. The glass of water tilts as the figure stands. We don't see the face.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A converted brownstone Noah uses for sensitive interviews. One room. A table, three chairs, no windows on this floor. Lena seated. Noah standing behind her, against the wall. Victoria Cross is brought in by one of Noah's people, not handcuffed, not coerced — but clearly not in control of the venue. She wears her usual charcoal Chanel-cut suit. The baroque pearls.

- CU Victoria's hand laying her purse on the table — careful

VICTORIA

Sit down. I'm going to show you what I have.

Then sit there. Did you authorize the surgery on Mara's face?

VICTORIA
(meeting her eyes)
No. I did not authorize the surgery. Look at me when I say it — I didn't.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]

## S#2. The admission · same room · minutes later

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- CU her hand sliding the page two inches across the wood
- MCU Lena waiting

VICTORIA

LENA
And the surgery.

VICTORIA
I didn't authorize it. I didn't even know about it until six months after it happened, by which point there was already a woman in the world who looked like you. I should have asked questions sooner. I didn't.

VICTORIA
The third trustee. The one your mother kept buried. I never met her. I only knew that she existed because Helena gave me one piece of paper the morning before she died.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.): Three people had held the key. Two were sitting in that room with me — the third was somewhere I hadn't looked yet.


## S#3. The folded paper · same room · the open

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria reaches into her purse. Pulls out a folded sheet of cream paper, twice. She has clearly been carrying it for a long time. She sets it on the table between them. Doesn't push it across yet.

- CU Victoria's hand resting on it
- MCU Lena, waiting, hands flat on the table

VICTORIA

I never looked. I told myself it was grief talking. I have spent four years pretending I never received this paper.

2. VICTORIA CROSS
3. EILEEN MARGAUX VOSS

Eileen.

I told you. I never knew her.

Eileen — that's the woman in the cemetery outside the city. The headstone Mara has been bringing flowers to. The mother on her birth certificate.

Lena, very quietly:

No. Eileen isn't dead. There's a body in a cemetery with her name on it, but there is somebody alive who has been moving Halcyon money for four years, and she signed her name on this paper the same year my mother died.

Noah steps forward from the wall.
