# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP33

## S#1. Sterling Holdings · executive floor · morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's office. Clean morning, soft light. Lena at her desk. Intercom: "Mr. Cross is here. He didn't have an appointment." Lena lifts her eyes from a folder, sets it face-down. Says into the intercom: "Five minutes. Then I have a call."

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena setting the folder down
- Wide of the doorway as Ethan enters
- OTS Lena → Ethan, two steps inside

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Don't sit down. Whatever you came to say — say it standing.

ETHAN
My mother told me this morning she's been sitting on your mother's paper for four years. I was raised in that house and I never noticed. I'm not here to ask you for anything. I came because I had every reason to recognize you in that event hall and I didn't, and that's mine to live with. I'm sorry. Properly sorry. The kind that doesn't ask anything back.

LENA
(after a long beat)
I accept that. You don't have to carry the part where you didn't recognize me anymore. Go. Live the rest of your life. And be careful what you tell your mother about my next move.

ETHAN
She told me you're going to the lake house tomorrow. She thought I should know in case it goes badly.

LENA
Then you've delivered both messages. Go, Ethan.

He nods once. Doesn't put out his hand. Walks out. The door closes behind him quieter than he came in.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.): For two years I had waited for him to say the small honest thing without dressing it up as a fight to win me back. He had finally said it, and I had less time for it than I'd thought I would.


## S#2. Mara's Apartment — Bathroom · same morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara at the bathroom sink. Cold water running. She's been splashing it on her face. She straightens, looks at herself in the mirror — surgical scar two centimeters under her left ear catching the overhead light. The face she has been wearing for two years looks back. She lifts her hair off her neck. Sets it down again. Picks up her phone off the counter.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the surgical scar in the mirror
- MCU Mara, half her face wet
- CU her hand picking up the phone

[INTERCUT]
INTERCUT — Lena, in her office, looking at the closed door Ethan just walked out of. Then back to Lena answering a call from a number she does not recognize.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (V.O., on the line)
Don't say my name. Don't react. Are you alone in your office.

LENA
Yes.

MARA (V.O.)
I went to the cemetery yesterday. I sat at your mother's grave and I asked her to tell me which woman I came from. She didn't answer. So I'm asking you. I don't think I came from her. I don't think I ever did.

LENA
What changed.

MARA (V.O.)
I called my own mother last night to ask about the day I was born. She didn't pick up. Then she called me back two hours later from a number I didn't know, and she told me a story about the hospital that I'd never heard before in twenty-six years. She had to put it together in front of me. I sat on the bathroom floor when we got off the phone. Something is wrong with the woman who raised me, and I don't know what.


## S#3. Mara's Apartment — Bathroom · continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara, still at the sink, leans her free hand on the counter. The mirror is fogging slightly from the cold water she ran a minute ago. Her reflection holds, sharper than it should be.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Mara's hand white on the counter
- MCU Mara, phone at her ear, no makeup
- OTS Mara → her own reflection

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
What do you want to do.

MARA (V.O.)
I want to come to the lake house tomorrow the way you asked me to. Just you and me. But I need you to know — if you have something to tell me, I'm not bringing her into the room with me anymore. I'm coming with my own ears on.

LENA
Then come alone.

MARA (V.O.)
I will.

She ends the call. Sets the phone face down on the marble. Looks back at the mirror. The hand near her scar comes up almost on its own and rests there — fingertips flat against the curved white line — as if she is meeting it for the first time.

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I had spent two years learning to call a stranger my mother. I had spent twenty-six learning to call a woman in a small apartment mine. I was starting to understand that one of those numbers was wrong. I did not yet know which.


## S#4. Sterling Holdings · Lobby · afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Ground-floor lobby of Sterling Holdings. Late afternoon foot traffic — employees crossing the marble toward the revolving door. On the long bench by the elevators, a WOMAN in her late fifties — gray-streaked dark hair pinned tight, a wide-brimmed hat in her lap, a folded coat across her knees. She's been there ten minutes. Calm. She's reading the building directory on her phone.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the lobby, the woman small at the edge of frame
- MCU the woman, face partially obscured by the hat brim
- CU her hand on the phone — Lena's name highlighted in the directory
- ECU her left thumb where a bone bend across the joint is just visible

Same thumb bend. Match unmistakable to the audience. Lena does not see it. Ethan, somewhere upstairs, does not know the photograph exists.

[END HOOK]
The woman on the bench presses the elevator call button. The doors open. She steps in. The doors close on her face — calm, unhurried, lipstick fresh. The directory display above her ticks one floor up, then another, then another. She is not stopping at Lena's floor. She is going higher. To a floor Lena's company doesn't own.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP34

## S#1. The Lake House · gravel drive · 2:50 p.m.

[VISUAL/ACTION]

She wears the slate coat. She does not look up the slope behind the house, where the gravel path bends out of sight into trees. We know Noah is there.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A wood-floored living room. Stone fireplace, no fire. Mara is already there, standing by the window facing the lake. She turns when she hears Lena's boots on the floor. They look at each other across ten feet of empty room. Same face. Different posture entirely — Mara is slightly forward on her toes, alert in a way she wasn't four months ago. Lena is steady, hands at her sides.

- Wide of the room, both women in frame, ten feet between them
- MCU Mara at the window
- MCU Lena in the doorway
- ECU the surgical scar 2cm below Mara's left ear, visible because her hair is up

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You came alone.

MARA
You asked me to come alone. So I did.

LENA
Sit down. Please.

MARA
I'm fine standing.

LENA
Then I'll stand too.


## S#3. The truth · same room · the body of the scene

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena keeps her coat on. Doesn't move closer than the corner of the couch. She gives Mara the truth in the same order she gave it to Ethan yesterday — paragraphs, not bullets.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena, even light from the lake window
- MCU Mara, profile, jaw tight
- CU Mara's hand at her side, fingers slowly opening and closing
- OTS Lena → Mara

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You're not my mother's daughter, Mara. I have her hospital records, her trust documents, the birth certificate with Julian Voss and Eileen Voss on it, and my family doctor's testimony from the night you were born. My mother lost a baby that night. Three floors up from yours. That part was real. But the child she lost wasn't you.

LENA
Julian is your father. Eileen is your mother. You were raised believing your father was someone else because somebody paid Julian to disappear before you were old enough to remember him. That somebody was my mother. I'm not defending what she did. I'm telling you what she did.

LENA
But you weren't hers. You were never hers. The story you've been told for the last two years — that you were my mother's hidden child, that you were owed a place inside this family — that wasn't her doing. She was already dead.

[BEAT]

LENA
Someone has been lying to you. Someone wanted you to come for me. And it worked.

[VO]
MARA (V.O., low, almost stripped of the trained-Lena cadence she's been wearing for twenty-five episodes — faster, slightly more anxious, her own voice underneath): I knew. Somewhere I knew, and I kept walking forward anyway.


## S#4. The question · same room · five minutes later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara has not moved from the window. She has been listening with her whole body. Now she finally lets her hand fall flat against the glass behind her.

- MCU Mara, the lake behind her out of focus
- OTS Mara → Lena, finally

MARA
By who.

By the person who has had the power to move my mother's contingency money for the last four years. The third trustee. The one who paid for your surgery without telling Julian. The one who has been feeding you a version of my mother's life that wasn't true.

MARA
You have a name.

LENA
I have a name. I'm going to say it slowly because I need you to hear who I think has been on the other end of this with you.

MARA
Say it.

LENA
Eileen.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara's hand slides down the glass two inches. She doesn't speak. The room is very quiet.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara's phone, set face-down on the coffee table, begins to vibrate. It buzzes once, twice, three times. Mara doesn't move toward it. Lena watches it. Mara picks it up. Looks at the screen. Doesn't answer at first.

- OTS Lena → Mara

(eyes locked on Lena)

[END HOOK]

WOMAN
Walk out the front door, Mara. Right now. I am closer than you think.

The call stays open. Wind outside the lake house. Noah on the ridge, watching the front door through the lens. Lena and Mara, both still standing, the phone between them.

(barely audible)
Milo.

She wouldn't hurt him.

LENA
She is sitting in your apartment with a driver in your kitchen. Listen to your own voice ask that question.

[VO]

I had spent eight months learning who put her face on top of mine. I had not spent one minute thinking about who put the idea in her head in the first place. The whole time there was a woman behind her, holding the leash.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP36

## S#1. Noah's Office · Pre-Dawn

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah's office, every overhead light on. The wall behind his desk is now a paper grid — photocopies, ledger pages, a faded employment photograph from a chemistry laboratory in the late 1990s. LENA sits in the leather chair across from him in jeans and a black sweater, her mother's necklace still at her throat. NOAH stands at the wall with a marker. A police scanner on his credenza is whispering — Mara was picked up safe at her apartment four hours ago, Milo unharmed, Eileen gone before the unit arrived.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the wall of paper, Noah small against it
- ECU the chemistry lab photo — a young woman, late twenties, dark hair, plain coat, no jewelry
- MCU Lena reading from a printed page in her lap
- CU the marker tip touching the photograph

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
That's her. Junior research assistant in your grandmother's polymer lab, 1997. Fired in 1999. The personnel file uses the word misconduct twice and doesn't explain it. The legal file in the next drawer over does — she walked out with about four years of research notebooks and tried to publish under her own name.

LENA
And my grandmother bought her silence.

NOAH
Paid her off, set her up with a small annuity through what later became the Halcyon Trust, and made her sign an NDA that's still enforceable on paper. Your grandmother died, your mother inherited the obligation, and your mother — for reasons I think had more to do with guilt than anything else — kept the annuity flowing instead of fighting it.

LENA
My mother was not afraid of a fight. She kept it flowing for a different reason.

NOAH
I think you're right. I don't know that reason yet. But the annuity ran without a problem for fifteen years. Then Eileen had a daughter with Julian Voss, and the moment that daughter turned about fifteen, the withdrawals on the trust start looking different. Bigger. Stranger purposes. Tuition that wasn't tuition. Medical that wasn't medical.

LENA
Surgery.

NOAH
The first cosmetic withdrawal traces back nine years ago. Small one. The big ones, the face work, start three years ago.

## S#2. Noah's Office · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena gets up, crosses to the wall, looks at the lab photograph closely for the first time. The young Eileen in the picture has Mara's exact jawline. Lena does not touch the photo. She steps back.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Lena → the photograph
- ECU Eileen's young face, then pull to current age comparison Noah tacks beside it (a recent surveillance frame: late-50s woman in silver-grey wool coat over a plain dress, hands in her pockets, no jewelry, walking out of a courthouse cafe)
- MCU Lena's reaction — quiet, not theatrical

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
She has Mara's face — which means Mara has hers. There was never any version of this where Mara was mine.

NOAH
No. There wasn't.

LENA
And my mother. She didn't know.

NOAH
She knew Eileen had a daughter. The annuity paperwork lists Mara as a dependent from age four. Your mother never met the child. She wrote a check, signed a form, and moved on. Eileen took those forms and built a whole second life inside them. Your mother had no idea she was funding it.

LENA
She would have hated knowing that. She would have considered it her fault.

NOAH
That's probably why Eileen kept it quiet. A confrontation would have cut the money off.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother kept too many quiet kindnesses going past their expiration date. She always said you don't punish a stranger for being inconvenient. I think she paid that woman every month for thirty years because she thought it was the right thing to do, and a woman with a long enough memory turned the right thing into a weapon.

## S#3. Eileen Voss's Apartment Building — Lobby · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A modest brick walk-up in a quiet residential block. Mara, in plain clothes, no makeup, surgical scar visible under her ear in daylight for the first time in years, comes through the front door with a building superintendent. She holds a small key. Police tape was up overnight and has been pulled down. Eileen is gone — somewhere in the city, on the move. Mara has come back, alone, to look for something.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the lobby — Mara dwarfed in it
- MCU the super opening the apartment door for her
- OTS Mara stepping into the apartment

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
You don't have to come up. I'm only here for ten minutes.

SUPER
The officer said somebody should be on the floor with you.

MARA
The officer is on the next landing. I won't be a problem, Mr. Park. I grew up coming in and out of this hallway. I know the carpet.

She lets herself into Eileen's apartment alone.

## S#4. Eileen Voss's Apartment — Bedroom Closet · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The apartment is too tidy. Bed made tight, kitchen wiped down, no photographs on the walls. Mara goes straight to the bedroom closet and pulls down a shoebox from a high shelf. It is sealed with old packing tape. She sits on the floor with her back against the closet wall, slits the tape with a key, opens the lid.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU her hands lifting the box down
- CU the tape parting under the key
- OTS Mara → the open box
- ECU what's inside: a stack of school notebooks, one of them red

[GRAPHIC/UI]
The red notebook open in her lap. Spiral-bound. Childhood handwriting on most pages, careful loops, dotted i's. But one entry, dated when she would have been fourteen, is in a different hand — adult, slanted, slightly tighter. The page reads:

> If she finds the photo of Helena pregnant, run with it. Tell her she's been lied to. Make her believe she was the one rejected. Hold it on her until she stops asking questions about her own father. — Plan A.

The signature line at the bottom is not childlike. It is an adult initial: E.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(reading aloud to no one, voice gone very flat)
Plan A.

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I have spent eleven years thinking I broke open a secret. I sat with that diary in a closet when I was fifteen and I cried for a week and I made a promise to myself that I was going to walk back into Helena's life and be the daughter she should have had. I thought I cracked the lock on my own. She wrote the lock and the key and she left them in the box for me to find.

## S#5. Eileen Voss's Apartment — Bedroom Closet · Moments Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara closes the notebook slowly. Sets it down beside her. Opens her phone, takes a photograph of the page. Then takes another, just in case. She sits a long moment with both hands flat on her thighs.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU the camera shutter sound, screen flash twice
- MCU Mara sitting on the closet floor
- ECU her thumb hovering over Lena's name in her contacts list

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(quietly)
Lena.

She does not call. She closes the contact. She gets up, takes the shoebox under her arm, and walks out.

[END HOOK]
Mara at the apartment doorway, looking back into the rooms she grew up visiting. She turns the light off and pulls the door shut. In the hallway her phone buzzes — a message from a private number.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
UNKNOWN: I see you went back. Good. Bring the box to the address I sent. Don't bring the man's wife. — Mom.

Mara reads it once. She does not reply. She puts the phone in her coat pocket and walks down the stairs.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP37

## S#1. Storage Office Above a Dry Cleaner — Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A second-floor office above a corner dry cleaner in a quiet part of town. Steam vent on the side wall. Mara comes up the back stairs alone, shoebox under one arm, the red notebook in her free hand. She does not knock. She pushes the door open. EILEEN VOSS — a woman in her late fifties, identical eye color and jawline to Mara, silver-grey wool coat over a plain charcoal dress, no jewelry, hands always in her pockets — stands at a window with her back to the room. She does not turn until Mara has closed the door.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the small office, Eileen silhouetted at the window
- MCU Mara stopping six feet inside the door
- ECU the shoebox under Mara's arm
- OTS Eileen turning halfway, the matching jaw catching the steam-grey light

[DIALOGUE]
EILEEN
You came. I knew you would. Set the box down on the desk. We have less time than I'd like for this conversation, so please don't make me ask twice.

MARA
I came because I want to hear you say it out loud. Not on a phone. Not behind anything. Sit down at that desk and tell me what you've been doing for twenty years.

EILEEN
(after a beat, turning fully)
All right. Sit. I'll tell you anything you ask.

She walks calmly to the small desk, pulls out one of two chairs, sits.

## S#2. Storage Office · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara stays standing. She sets the shoebox on the desk between them but keeps the notebook in her hand. The light from the window puts both their faces in the same grey wash — the same jaw, the same eyes, twenty-five years apart.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot across the desk
- ECU the notebook in Mara's hand, page already marked
- CU Eileen's hands folded neatly on the desk — no rings, no jewelry, the only adornment the slight bend of an old fracture across her left thumb

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
You wrote this in my diary when I was fourteen. You waited two months and then you walked me into that closet and you watched me find it. You stood in the doorway. I remember thinking you were upset for me. You weren't upset. You were checking your work.

EILEEN
I was checking my work. I will not pretend with you. You came here for the truth and I owe you the truth.

MARA
Why.

EILEEN
Because I gave you a purpose. I gave you a name to want and a woman to want to become and an enemy worth being angry at for half your life. You are sitting in this chair as a person because of that purpose. Tell me honestly — without it, what would you have been. A surgeon's assistant in a borrowed apartment. A girl with a pretty face who shared a couch with a boy named Milo and went to bed early. I gave you a destiny. You think you would have built one yourself.

MARA
(voice flat, very level)
I had a name. It was Mara Voss. My father was Julian. My mother was the woman who used to walk me to school. You took that name out of my mouth and you taught me to be embarrassed of it. You taught me to spell Sterling before I could spell my own.

EILEEN
And now you want it back. Mara. My love. It's late. The last move is already made.

## S#3. Storage Office · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Eileen reaches across the desk and turns on the small television sitting at the end of it. She has been waiting for this. The picture comes up on a live news feed — a financial reporter standing in front of Sterling Holdings tower, lower-third graphic already loaded.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the remote in Eileen's hand
- Two-shot Mara and Eileen, the TV in the foreground, light shifting on their faces
- CU the news graphic

[GRAPHIC/UI]
NEWS LOWER THIRD:
BREAKING — STERLING HOLDINGS' HALCYON TRUST UNDER FEDERAL REVIEW. ANONYMOUS SOURCE ALLEGES LENA STERLING ROUTED PRIVATE FAMILY FUNDS THROUGH CHARITABLE VEHICLE FOR YEARS.

The reporter cuts to an audio clip — a woman's voice, manipulated, slightly digital around the edges:

> "I worked closely with Ms. Sterling for two years. The money never went where the trust said it went. I have records. I'm coming forward because someone has to."

The cadence of the voice is almost — almost — Mara's.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
That isn't me.

EILEEN
It doesn't have to be you. It only has to sound like you for one news cycle. By the time anyone proves it isn't, she will have lost the company's confidence, the cousins' votes, and her seat on the Halcyon board. And once she loses that board seat, my name is no longer the third trustee on anything. The paperwork unwinds.

MARA
You made them clone my voice.

EILEEN
I made them lift forty seconds of you from an interview you gave when you were twenty-four and rearrange the syllables. It cost less than my rent. Sit down, Mara. Watch the rest of it with me. This is the part I have been working toward since you were five years old.

## S#4. Storage Office · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara does not sit. She lifts the red notebook from the desk and tucks it inside her coat. Then, slowly, she reaches for the shoebox. Eileen watches her.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU Mara's hand on the shoebox
- ECU Eileen's eyes — calm, almost amused, no panic
- MCU Mara pulling the box back toward herself

[DIALOGUE]
EILEEN
You're going to take it to her. I assumed you would. That's why I made my move first. She'll be too busy keeping her own name out of the dirt to do anything useful with it. You will get there with your little box of proof and find her on a phone with a lawyer, and you will discover what I always knew — that woman is a brand more than she is a person, and brands defend themselves before they defend their friends.

MARA
You don't know her.

EILEEN
I know everything about her that her mother wrote in a Christmas card. I know more about her than you do. Sit down.

MARA
No. I'm going to walk out this door, drive across the bridge, and put this box in her hands. When she opens it she is going to do exactly what you've spent twenty years training me to assume nobody like her would ever do. She'll put the lawyer down. And she'll pick this up.

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
For half my life I had lived inside the story this woman wrote for me. Every time I felt small I went back to it, and it made me feel chosen. Standing in that room I was realizing the story had been the smallest thing about me — a leash, not a destiny.

## S#5. Storage Office · Doorway

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara reaches the door with the shoebox in one arm. She turns back. Eileen has not stood.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara at the door
- OTS Mara → Eileen at the desk
- ECU Eileen's hand reaching for the desk phone

[DIALOGUE]
EILEEN
(quiet, not looking up)
You'll come back. You always have.

MARA
I know I always have. That's how I know this is the last time.

[END HOOK]
Mara opens the door, steps through, pulls it shut behind her. On the television behind Eileen the fake clip is looping — the voice that almost sounds like Mara reading allegations off the air. Eileen lifts the desk phone, dials a single number from memory, says one word into it:

EILEEN
Move.

Cut to Lena's office, half a city away — every screen on the wall is showing the same news story. Lena is already on her feet, coat in her hand, Noah at the door.

LENA
Get the press call ready. We're going to give them the whole file. Today.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP38

## S#1. Lena's Apartment — Study · Late Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's small home study, the city light failing through the long window, a single desk lamp on. LENA is alone at the desk in a charcoal turtleneck, her mother's necklace at her throat, the Halcyon Trust binder open in front of her — the only thing in the room with her. The TV in the next room is muted but visible through the doorway, the manipulated audio clip from EP37 looping on three different cable feeds. She is not looking at it. She has been on this same page of the binder for twenty minutes — a quarterly wire transfer dated 2010, the recipient line typed in her mother's careful hand: *E. VOSS — STIPEND. CONTINGENCY TRUSTEE THIRD POSITION.*

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the study, the muted feeds in the next room behind her
- MCU Lena at the desk, finger resting on the page
- ECU the page — *E. VOSS — STIPEND. CONTINGENCY TRUSTEE THIRD POSITION.*
- CU her hand sliding the binder closed, then opening it again

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother had written it down sixteen years before I would need to read it. She had paid Eileen Voss a quarterly stipend for as long as I had been alive — not as a kindness, as a leash. And she had named her in the trust the same week. I had been reading that name on this page for an hour. I had been deciding what to do with it.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She closes the binder. She picks up her phone. She dials Bannon — the call we don't hear. She speaks one full sentence into it.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into the phone, level)
I'm opening the trust. The full audit. Voluntary, public, every wire, every name. Eileen Voss in front of the country by tomorrow morning. Yes — I'm sure. Send a car at seven.

(she ends the call)

She lays the phone face-down on the binder. She does not move for a count.


## S#2. Sterling Holdings — Press Briefing Room · Next Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A modest briefing room on the ground floor, fifty chairs, twelve of them filled with credentialed press. No banner, no step-and-repeat. Two cameras at the back. LENA at the lectern in a midnight-blue suit, her mother's necklace visible at her collar, hair pulled back. MR. BANNON — sixties, the family attorney, charcoal three-piece — is seated to one side. The disclosure binder is open on a side table behind her, courier-stamped this morning. Noah stands at the back wall, near the door, arms crossed.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the press room from the lectern POV
- MCU Lena at the lectern, both hands resting on the wood
- CU Noah at the back, watching the room more than the speaker
- ECU the Halcyon Trust binder open on the side table

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Yesterday a clip went out under my name. The voice on it is not mine. I'm not going to spend this morning telling you what it isn't. I'd rather spend it showing you what it is. As of this morning the Halcyon Trust — which my mother founded in 2009 and which I inherited as a fiduciary at her death — is open. Every document on the side table behind me. Online in an hour. I want you to read it before you ask me about any of it.

(she lifts one page from the binder without looking down at it)

There is a name in those documents I'd like to put on the record myself, so you don't have to find it. The third trustee listed in the contingency section of the Halcyon Trust is a woman named Eileen Voss. My mother named her in 2010 as a condition of a private stipend that the trust had been paying to her every quarter for sixteen years. There is also a wire transfer dated April 2022 that paid for a cosmetic surgery performed on a young woman named Mara Voss — Eileen Voss's daughter — to make her face resemble mine. That wire is in the binder too.

(beat)

I'm going to say one thing about Mara Voss and then I'd like to not say her name again from this lectern. Mara Voss has been used by her own mother for fifteen years. She is not the architect of what was done to me. I'm not going to pile a third lie onto her. Eileen Voss is the architect. That's what you need to hear today.

(she closes the binder)

I'll take three questions.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
Lower-third graphic on a feed in the corner monitor: LENA STERLING — STERLING HOLDINGS — STATEMENT ON HALCYON TRUST


## S#3. Bannon's Sedan · Twenty Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The back of a quiet black sedan moving away from the press building. Bannon at the door, his window already up. Lena in the back seat alone now, coat across her lap, the binder beside her on the leather. Noah is two cars behind in his own car — visible once in the side mirror.

- MCU Lena in the back seat, head tipped against the window
- ECU her hand on the closed binder
- INSERT — side mirror, Noah's car following

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The audit had gone live online while I was at the lectern. Three hundred pages, every quarter, every wire, sixteen years of my mother's handwriting on the recipient lines. I had stopped being the only person who knew Eileen Voss's name. The audit would do the rest of the work for me by lunch.


## S#4. Eileen's Apartment · Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
INTERCUT — a small downtown apartment, immaculate, almost empty of personal objects. The television is on, tuned to the live feed. EILEEN VOSS sits in a low armchair facing the screen, silver-grey wool coat folded across her lap as if she has not bothered to put it away, hands in her coat pockets even while seated. She watches Lena's face on the screen without moving for the full length of the statement.

- ECU her eyes — no movement, no narrowing, no widening

- CU Eileen's hand, slow, lifting from the coat pocket and resting on the armrest

[DIALOGUE]
EILEEN
(to no one, quiet, two full sentences)
She named me at her own lectern before lunch. I underestimated how much of her mother she actually kept.


## S#5. Coffee Shop · Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A small coffee shop two blocks from Mara's apartment, mostly empty at mid-morning. MARA sits at a corner table in a dark wool coat, her hair tied back, the shoebox from EP37 on the seat next to her. A small wall-mounted television above the counter is carrying the press conference, sound low. The barista is the only other person in the shop. Mara has not touched her coffee.

I had been ready for her to put a knife in me at that lectern this morning. I had a sentence prepared for the moment she did. The sentence has nowhere to go now.

(to the barista, quiet)

(picking up)
Mara.

Where are you. I'm coming in. I want to give you what I have.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP39

## S#1. Glenmoor Cemetery — Sterling Family Plot · Sunset

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A quiet hillside cemetery above the river, the low sun catching the white marble of the headstones. Helena Sterling's marker is at the end of a short gravel path, a modest white stone with cut letters and a clean rectangle of grass in front of it. LENA waits at the foot of the stone in a long dark coat over the midnight-blue suit from the morning, her mother's necklace at her throat. NOAH stands at the edge of the path, maybe fifteen yards back, hands in his pockets, eyes on the access road behind them. The wind moves the trees but not much else.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the path from the access road
- MCU Lena at the foot of the headstone
- OTS Lena → the headstone, Helena's name visible
- CU Noah at the path edge, scanning

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
She'll come up the south path. The one without the lights. That's the route I'd choose if I didn't want anyone behind me.

LENA
Stay where you are. If she stops on the path I'll go to her. I don't want her thinking we set this up to surround her.

NOAH
I'll be where you can see me.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother used to bring me here on the anniversary of her own mother. We never said much. She would put one hand on the stone and stand there and breathe. I came up here with her every year and I never once asked what she was thinking about. I think today I'm going to find out.


## S#2. The Path · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
MARA appears at the bend in the south path. She is in a long dark coat, her hair tied back, carrying a small wrapped bundle of white peonies in her left hand and the shoebox under her right arm. She stops twenty paces short of the gravesite when she sees Lena. The two of them look at each other across the gravel. Then Mara starts walking again. She comes the rest of the way alone. Noah does not move from his post.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Long lens down the path, Mara approaching
- MCU Mara walking, the peonies steady in her hand
- ECU her wrist — the faint surgical scar on the inner side catching the low sun
- Two-shot Lena and Mara as Mara stops three feet from the stone

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
I brought peonies. She liked these, didn't she. I read it in a magazine profile of her once when I was sixteen and I never forgot it.

LENA
She liked white ones. You got the right color.

MARA
(kneeling once, briefly, laying the flowers on the grass at the base of the stone, then standing again)
I wasn't sure if you'd want me to put them down. I didn't want to assume anything.

LENA
You can put flowers on my mother's grave. That's allowed.


## S#3. Gravesite · The Handoff

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara shifts the shoebox into both hands. She holds it for a beat, then sets it carefully on the flat top of the headstone — not on the grass, not in Lena's hands, on the stone itself, like she is putting it where neither of them can pretend it isn't there. She opens the lid. Inside, on top of the older diary pages and the bracelet and a folded clinic invoice, is a leather-bound notebook tied with a thin black cord.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the shoebox lid coming up
- OTS Mara → the contents
- CU Lena's hands, still at her sides, not yet reaching for it
- ECU the leather notebook, the cord around it

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
This is the one I didn't show you when we talked on the phone this morning. It was in her storage office. She left it on the desk where I would find it. I think she wanted me to read it before I made up my mind which side of this I was on. She thought reading it would scare me into staying. It didn't.

LENA
(reaching for the notebook now, untying the cord, opening it on the headstone)
What am I looking at.

MARA
It's a two-year plan. It's annotated. You'll see her handwriting in the margins. The first half is what you already know — the surgery, the wedding to Ethan, the trust position. The back half is what you don't. There is a contingency in there for after.

LENA
After what.

MARA
After you. There is a section that begins with the word "if." It assumes that at some point in the first two years of the marriage you would no longer be a problem. I assume she planned to arrange that. She had a DNA proof drawn up for me that I had never seen until I opened that book — a manufactured one, with the right name on it. Once you were gone I was supposed to step into the inheritance with the paper she'd manufactured. Then I was supposed to marry Ethan for the cover. Then, when I was no longer useful, she was supposed to dispose of me as well. She'd already chosen the method. It's on the last page.

(beat)

I have known this since the morning I found my own diary entry in her handwriting. I have been carrying it around for a week. I wanted to give it to you in person because I didn't want you to find it the way I did. Alone, in a closet.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the page of the notebook, the word "if" visible at the top of a section
- MCU Lena reading, very still
- CU Mara watching Lena's face

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
There is a particular silence that happens when you read a sentence in someone else's handwriting that describes the end of your own life. The wind keeps going. The trees keep moving. None of it lands on you. You stand inside that silence and you wait for it to mean something. It takes a minute.


## S#4. Gravesite · Mara at the Stone

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena closes the notebook. She does not put it back in the box. She holds it under one arm. Mara has turned away from her, toward the headstone, and stands with her hands loose at her sides, looking at Helena's name cut into the marble.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Mara → the cut letters of Helena's name
- MCU Mara from the side
- CU Lena watching her

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
I'm not going to talk to her. I don't have the right to talk to a dead woman whose name I rented for two years. I wanted to stand here long enough to know that I understood what I'd done. I think I'm starting to. The thing I keep coming back to is that I was a person before any of this started. I had an apartment. I had a job. I had a man named Milo who'd come over on a Friday and make me eat something that wasn't from a vending machine. I didn't know that was a life. I thought it was the waiting room before my real life started. She'd taught me that. She'd taught me to look at the small things I had and think of them as a hallway. I'm trying to learn how to look at a Friday night with a friend and call it a Friday night.

(she does not turn her head)

I don't have an apology that's the size of what I did. I'm not going to try to fit one into this conversation. I'd rather hand you the book and let you decide what comes next.


## S#5. Gravesite · Lena's Answer

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena moves around to stand beside Mara at the foot of the headstone. She does not put her hand on the stone the way her mother used to. She puts the notebook on top of the shoebox lid, gently, like she's closing a door.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot Lena and Mara at the foot of the stone
- ECU Lena's hand resting on the closed shoebox
- MCU Lena's face in the low light
- OTS Lena → Mara

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(after a long beat)
We're going to stop her. I'm going to read every page of that notebook tonight and I'm going to take it to Bannon in the morning and we're going to use it the way she meant us to use it against each other, except we're going to use it against her instead. Then I'm going to make sure you have somewhere to land that isn't a courtroom.

MARA
Why.

LENA
Because she made you into a weapon. You used the weapon. But you aren't the weapon. I'm not going to pretend you didn't pick it up. I'm also not going to pretend she didn't put it in your hand when you were five.

MARA
That's a kinder answer than I'd prepared for.

LENA
I prepared a harder one. I don't think I need it.


## S#6. Gravesite · The Phone

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The low sun is almost gone. Noah has not moved from the path. Mara reaches into her coat pocket for a tissue, and her phone — which she has set to silent — vibrates against her hand. She pulls it out, looks at the screen. The screen reads, in caps: MOTHER. She presses the side button to decline the call without answering. She looks up at Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the phone screen — MOTHER
- CU Mara's thumb on the side button
- MCU Mara looking up
- Wide of the gravesite, the light almost gone

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
She knows I'm here.

LENA
(immediately, calm, already lifting her own phone)
Noah. We're walking out together. Take the long way to the car.

NOAH
(from the path, already moving)
Copy. Stay close.

[END HOOK]
Mara picks up the shoebox. Lena tucks the leather notebook under her arm. They start down the path together, Noah falling in behind them three paces back. Hold on the headstone — the white peonies on the grass, the cut letters of Helena Sterling's name, the last of the sunset hitting the marble. In the distance, beyond the cemetery gate, a black sedan pulls slowly out of a side road and turns to follow at a distance. The dashboard camera light blinks once, then goes dark.

Hard Cut.

The same downtown apartment from EP38 — sparse, immaculate, the television still tuned to the live feed but now muted. The lower-third graphic on the screen has changed: HALCYON TRUST DISCLOSURE FILE NOW PUBLIC — EILEEN VOSS NAMED. EILEEN sits at the small desk in the corner, hands folded on the wood, the silver-grey wool coat still over her shoulders even indoors. A small burner phone sits in front of her, face-up. She is not packing. She is not pacing.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the apartment, the muted TV behind her
- MCU Eileen at the desk
- ECU her hands folded — no rings, no jewelry, the old fracture across her left thumb
- INSERT: the TV chyron with her name on it

[VO]
EILEEN (V.O.)
The first thing you learn, doing what I do, is that there is a moment between the loss of a plan and the start of the next one, and the moment is shorter than people think. My daughter spent the afternoon at a gravesite handing my book to the woman I taught her to hate. I am not going to spend tonight feeling that. I am going to spend tonight cleaning.

[DIALOGUE]
EILEEN
(picking up the burner, dialing one number from memory)
It's me. I'd like you to do one more thing for me tonight. I'll send the address. Bring a second man. Bring something quiet for the door. I don't want it to look like anything when you're done.

(beat, listening)

The price is the same as last time. Half on arrival, half when you confirm. You can stop by the storage office for the first half before you start. The envelope is on the desk under the green book.

She ends the call. She lays the burner down. She does not move for a long count.


## S#2. Mara's Apartment · Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
INTERCUT — Mara's small one-bedroom apartment, third floor of a converted brownstone. Books on the floor along one wall. A lamp on a side table, the only light in the room. The shoebox from the gravesite sits on the kitchen counter, the leather notebook gone with Lena. Mara is at the small kitchen sink, running her right hand under cold water. The skin across her knuckles is scraped raw — from the coffee shop yesterday, when she pushed the door open with the back of her hand because she couldn't make herself grip the handle. MILO HAYES — twenty-five, surgical assistant build, lean and a little tired around the eyes, in a soft blue henley — stands beside her with a small bandage roll, unbothered by her hands wet on his sleeve.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara at the sink, Milo at her elbow
- ECU her knuckles under the running water, the small scrapes
- CU Milo's hands unrolling the bandage
- Two-shot at the sink, the lamp light warm on their faces

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
You really don't have to do this. I can wrap a hand. I do this for a living. Did.

MILO
You can wrap a hand on someone else. You're allowed to let me do yours. Hold still.

MARA
Milo. I want to say something and I want you to not interrupt me.

MILO
Go ahead.

MARA
You've been the only person in the last year who didn't ask me to be someone else. I had a friend named Tessa who'd been asking me to be Lena since we were nineteen and I didn't know that was what she was doing until it was already done. You met me when I was already pretending to be a woman whose voice I had trained to sound right in my mouth, and you never once asked me to do the voice. You let me eat takeout in a sweatshirt and read paperbacks on the floor. I don't think I ever told you what that was. I'd like to tell you now.

MILO
(working the bandage around her knuckles, eyes on his hands)
You're telling me now. I'm listening.

MARA
That was the only part of last year that was a life. The rest of it was a job. I'm trying to learn the difference. I'm not very good at it yet.

MILO
You don't have to be good at it tonight. Hold still. I'm going to tape this corner down and then we can eat the noodles.


## S#3. Mara's Apartment · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A floorboard creaks in the hallway outside the apartment. Not someone walking past — someone who has just stopped. Mara's head comes up. She knows the building. The bandage roll is still in Milo's hand. He notices her face change before he hears the second step. The chain lock on the apartment door is not engaged.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Mara's eyes flicking to the apartment door
- CU Milo following her look, half a beat behind
- INSERT: the apartment door, the chain hanging loose against the frame
- MCU Mara already moving past Milo toward the door

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(low, fast)
Milo. Back hallway. Now. Go down the service stairs to the laundry room and out the side. Don't take the front.

MILO
Mara—

MARA
I'm not asking. Go.

The apartment door opens before Milo has cleared the kitchen. Two men in dark coats, no faces we register, the lead one already crossing the room. Milo turns to come back, to put himself between them and Mara, and the second man takes him at the shoulder, hard, and walks him out into the hallway. Mara starts toward them and the lead man holds out one open palm.

LEAD MAN
We're not here for you. We're here for him. If you follow us down the stairs, that changes.

He follows the second man out. The apartment door stands open on the empty hallway. Mara is alone in the kitchen. The bandage roll has fallen to the floor.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the open apartment doorway
- MCU Mara alone in the kitchen, the lamp still warm behind her
- ECU the bandage roll on the floor
- CU Mara's phone face-down on the counter, vibrating once


## S#4. Industrial Block — Old Cosmetic Clinic Property · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A row of low industrial buildings on the river side of the city. One of them — a defunct cosmetic-surgery clinic where Mara worked as an assistant before everything started — is half-condemned, the front windows boarded. Mara pulls up in her own car, headlights cutting the empty lot. She has driven here on instinct. The building's freight entrance, around the side, is the only door she knows from the inside. She gets out of the car, leaves the keys in the ignition, walks straight in through the freight bay door, which is open three feet.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the empty lot, Mara's car the only one
- MCU Mara at the freight bay, the door open just enough
- OTS Mara stepping into the building
- ECU her phone in her hand, no signal bar

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I knew this building from the inside before I knew Lena's name. I worked the front desk here for eight months when I was twenty-three. The freight elevator on the south wall used to stick between floors twice a week. If she sent a man here with Milo, this is where he would put him. I knew that the way I know my own handwriting.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(into the empty corridor, loud, no theater)
Milo. If you can hear me. I'm in the south corridor. I'm coming to the freight elevator.

(no answer)

The corridor is empty. Service lighting, half the bulbs out. Mara walks past the old reception desk, past the consultation rooms with the doors hanging open, around the corner to the freight elevator. The doors are open. Inside, on the floor of the cab, is Milo's phone. Nothing else. Mara steps into the elevator to pick it up. As she straightens, the doors slide shut behind her with a quiet hydraulic hiss. The cab does not move. From below the floor she hears the soft hiss of something being released into the shaft.


## S#5. Freight Elevator · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara stands very still in the closed cab. She presses the door-open button. Nothing. She presses the floor buttons. Nothing. Through the seam at the bottom of the doors, a thin pale haze begins to come up — smoke, not thick yet, but the smell behind it is wrong. She gets her phone out. One signal bar. She dials.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Mara's thumb on the door-open button, no response
- CU the thin haze coming up through the door seam
- MCU Mara in the closed cab, phone to her ear
- INSERT: Lena's phone screen, MARA flashing

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(when Lena picks up)
Lena. Listen carefully. I'm in the old Brandt clinic, south corridor, freight elevator. The doors won't open. There's smoke coming up the shaft from below. She sent two men. They took Milo. They sent Milo home an hour ago — I can see him on this phone he left here — and they brought me. She's not after me. She's after both of us. She's going to burn this whole thing and walk away clean.

INTERCUT TO LENA — already in the back of Noah's car, Noah driving, Lena's phone on speaker between her and the dashboard, the city lights moving across her face.

LENA
We're nineteen minutes out. Noah's already calling it in. Mara — I need you to lie down on the floor of that cab. The smoke is going to be heaviest at the top.

MARA
I know. I'm lying down. Stay on the phone.


## S#6. Lena's Car · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah takes a hard left onto the river road, the speedometer climbing. Lena keeps the phone in her left hand, Mara's voice low and steady on speaker. Lena's other phone rings. UNKNOWN NUMBER. She picks it up on her second handset.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Tight on Lena in the back seat, two phones, two faces
- ECU the unknown caller ID
- OTS Noah → the road
- INSERT: the freight elevator, Mara on the floor, the haze visible above her

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into the second phone)
Eileen.

EILEEN
(calm, the same level as in the storage office)
I am giving you a courtesy I would not normally extend. You have a choice in front of you in the next twenty minutes. You can come to the building where my daughter is and you can use whatever resource you have to get her out, and I will accept that as the end of our conversation and you will not hear from me again. Or you can take this call as my last warning, and you can turn your car around, and Mara will be a tragedy that no one in this city will be able to tie to a name they can prosecute.

LENA
(very level, no rise)
Eileen. If you hurt Mara, I will spend every dollar my mother left me hunting you until you die in a prison cell.

EILEEN
Mara was always going to die in a fire. The only question was whose. You're choosing to make it yours. I'd like you to remember that.

(she ends the call)

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's hand setting the second phone down on the seat beside her
- CU Lena's other hand still holding the phone with Mara on it
- MCU Noah's eyes flicking to her in the rearview mirror
- Wide of the river road, the industrial block visible in the distance

[END HOOK]
Lena lifts the first phone back to her ear. Mara's breath is shallow on the line. Lena does not speak for a beat. Then she does, two full sentences, low and steady.

LENA
Mara. We are eleven minutes out. Keep breathing low and slow and stay on this line. I am not hanging up until Noah has his hands on those doors.

Hold on Lena's face in the back seat. Hold on the industrial block coming up out of the dark. The smoke from one of the buildings is just visible against the city light.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP41

## S#1. Industrial Block — Old Cosmetic Clinic Property · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Two unmarked police vehicles and a fire-rescue truck pull into the empty lot, no sirens, only running lights. NOAH's car comes in last and parks crooked. He is out before it's fully stopped, LENA two paces behind him with the phone still pressed to her ear. A SERGEANT in a dark jacket meets them at the freight bay door. Smoke is visible at the seam of the roofline now, thin but unmistakable. Two firefighters are already at the freight elevator with a pry bar.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the lot, the unmarked vehicles, the running lights
- MCU Noah out of the car, moving toward the bay
- ECU Lena's phone in her hand, MARA still on the call
- OTS the sergeant briefing Noah

[DIALOGUE]
SERGEANT
We've got the building cleared on the ground floor. Smoke's coming from the sub-basement utility room. Looks deliberate — there's an accelerant smell. Your woman's in the elevator?

NOAH
South corridor. Freight elevator. We're going in.

SERGEANT
My men are already at the doors. Stay behind me until I say.

NOAH
(starting to move)
Mara — Lena's on the phone with you — we're inside. Twenty seconds.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
There is a particular sound a freight elevator makes when a pry bar gets under the door. It is the sound of a piece of metal that did not want to be moved being moved anyway. I will hear that sound the rest of my life and not flinch.


## S#2. South Corridor · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The corridor is hazier now. Two firefighters at the freight elevator, one of them with the pry bar set into the door seam, the other steadying him. Noah comes up behind them, takes a breath, sets his shoulder against the side of the door, and adds his weight to the leverage. Lena keeps to the wall, phone still at her ear, voice steady on it.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU the firefighters at the door, Noah arriving
- ECU the pry bar working into the seam
- CU Lena at the wall, phone to her ear
- OTS Noah pushing, the door beginning to move

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
(through his teeth, to the firefighter)
On three. One. Two—

The door slides a third of the way open. The smoke inside the cab is layered along the ceiling, MARA on the floor, on her side, breathing into the inside of her elbow. She lifts her head. She is conscious. Her eyes find Lena first.

LENA
(into the phone, then putting it down)
I see her.

She closes the phone, drops it into her coat pocket, and is past Noah and into the cab before the door is fully open. She gets one arm under Mara's shoulders. Noah is right behind her, taking the other side. Together they walk Mara out of the cab and down the corridor. Mara's legs are unsteady. She doesn't speak. She keeps her face turned against Lena's shoulder for the first ten paces.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the door opening, the smoke layer visible
- MCU Lena and Noah supporting Mara out of the cab
- ECU Mara's face — ash on her cheek, her eyes open
- OTS Lena → Mara as they walk her out


## S#3. Private Airfield — Service Gate · Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
INTERCUT — a small private airfield on the north side of the city. A black sedan pulls up to the service gate. EILEEN sits in the back, the silver-grey wool coat folded across her knees, a single small suitcase beside her, hands in her coat pockets. The driver leans out to speak to the gate attendant. Before the gate lifts, two unmarked sedans roll into the access road behind them and one in front. Plainclothes officers come out of all three. The driver puts both hands on the wheel without being asked.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the airfield gate at night
- MCU Eileen in the back seat
- ECU her hand sliding out of the coat pocket and resting on her knee
- OTS the lead officer at her window

[DIALOGUE]
LEAD OFFICER
Ma'am. Step out of the vehicle, please. Hands where I can see them.

EILEEN
(without rising)
I will. I'm not going to be difficult. May I ask under what charge.

LEAD OFFICER
Conspiracy. Arson. Solicitation. We'll go through it at the station. Step out.

Eileen lifts her suitcase by the handle. She steps out of the car. She holds her hands out, palms turned slightly up, the way a woman might if she were testing the rain. The officer turns her gently against the sedan and cuffs her.

EILEEN
I would like to call my attorney from the car. I'd prefer not to wait until we reach the station.

LEAD OFFICER
You'll have a phone call when we book you. Watch your head.

He puts his hand on the top of her head and guides her into the back of his sedan. The door closes. The convoy turns out of the access road and heads back into the city.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Eileen being walked to the unmarked car
- ECU the cuffs at her wrists, the silver-grey coat sleeve over them
- CU the officer's hand on the top of her head, gentle
- Wide of the convoy pulling out

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I would not get to see this part. I would learn about it later that night, from Noah, in two sentences. I would not need to see it. The image I would carry instead was the one I already had — a woman in a wool coat standing in front of a window with her back to me, planning the end of my life and her own daughter's at the same desk.


## S#4. Hospital — Private Room · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A small private room on the fourth floor of the hospital, soft overhead light. MARA is in the bed under a thin blanket, a clear oxygen mask over her nose and mouth, a pulse-ox clip on her finger. Her hair has been pushed back off her forehead, a small piece of ash still on her temple where nobody has cleaned it yet. LENA sits in the chair beside the bed. Noah is at the door, talking quietly to a nurse, then steps back to give them the room. He stays in the corridor.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the room, the lamp light low
- MCU Mara in the bed, the mask on
- ECU her hand on the blanket, the bandage Milo wrapped still on her knuckles
- OTS Lena in the chair beside the bed

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(quiet)
They've got you on oxygen for another four hours. Your lungs are okay. The doctor says they got there before any real damage. You'll be sore in the morning.

MARA
(through the mask, voice small)
Milo.

LENA
Milo is at home. Noah's people picked him up from his apartment. He's been on the phone with the nurses station every twenty minutes. He's going to come by tomorrow when they let him.

MARA
(after a beat, voice small)
She used him as bait. I let her.

LENA
You didn't let her. You walked into a trap because she gambled that you would. You also called me from inside it before the door had been closed for thirty seconds. That's not letting her.


## S#5. Hospital — Private Room · The Page

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena reaches into her coat, which is folded over the chair back behind her, and takes out a single sheet of paper. It is a clean printed page — no letterhead, no logo, just a single line of text near the top. She lays it on the bed beside Mara's hand. Mara turns her head to read it. The line says, in plain print: MARA EILEEN VOSS.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the printed sheet on the blanket
- CU the three names on the page
- OTS Mara → the page
- MCU Mara reading it

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
That's the name on your birth certificate. Bannon pulled it for me this evening. I wanted you to see it. It's been in a file for thirty years and nobody has ever shown it to you.

MARA
(reading it twice, then weakly)
I never liked the way she said my middle name. She said it like a leash. Eileen. Mara Eileen. Like she was reminding me whose I was.

LENA
Then drop it. You're allowed.

MARA
Mara Voss. That's enough. Mara Voss is enough.

LENA
Mara Voss is enough.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Mara's hand sliding across the sheet, covering the middle name with her fingers
- CU her eyes closing for a beat under the mask
- MCU Lena watching her

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I had been afraid of my own name for fifteen years. It came out lighter than I expected.


## S#6. Hospital — Private Room · Sleep

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The oxygen mask hisses gently. The lamp on the bedside table has been turned to its lowest setting. Mara's eyes are heavy. Lena sits forward in the chair, her elbows on her knees. Outside the room, through the half-open door, Noah is visible against the corridor wall, drinking coffee from a paper cup, watching the elevator.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara, eyes closing
- ECU her hand finding Lena's at the edge of the blanket
- CU Lena's fingers closing around Mara's
- OTS Lena → Mara

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(barely audible, two short sentences without theater)
If I make it through this, I want to start over. Mara Voss. Somewhere your mother's name doesn't reach.

LENA
You'll make it through this. We'll find a place.

[END HOOK]
Mara's breathing evens out. She's asleep. Lena doesn't move her hand. She sits forward in the chair, watching her sleep, her own face very still. Through the door, Noah glances in once, sees Lena hasn't moved, doesn't interrupt. He goes back to watching the elevator. Hold on the two hands at the edge of the blanket, one bandaged, one not. Hold on the printed page on the blanket beside them — MARA EILEEN VOSS — and Mara's fingers, even in sleep, still covering the middle name.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP42

## S#1. Noah's Apartment — Office Corner · Late Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The corner of NOAH's apartment that he uses as a workspace — a long oak table against the window, two monitors, a third smaller screen for the camera transfer. Two cardboard evidence boxes sit beside the chair, lids off, labeled in marker: VOSS — DEVICES. On the table, splayed out in tidy rows, are EILEEN's burner phones, a slim laptop, a leather address book, and a thick file folder of printed contacts. The afternoon light is the cold blue of late autumn. Noah is in a charcoal sweater, sleeves to the elbow, no coat. There is a coffee on the windowsill that he has not touched.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the workspace from the window side
- MCU Noah at the table, sleeves to the elbow
- ECU the address book open to a page of handwritten names
- INSERT: the laptop screen — a list of files labeled "Contingency / Press"

[VO]
NOAH (V.O.)
The first thing I learned in this work is that nobody who plans for fifteen years plans for a single point of failure. Eileen had a press list ready to go the moment she went down. Eight planted identities. Stories already written. Editors paid in advance to pick up the feed. The plan was simple. The minute she was arrested, every one of them was going to publish a piece on Lena. By morning, the country would be reading three different versions of the same lie. My job tonight is to get to all eight before any of them know we have the list.

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
(into a headset, to an unseen assistant)
Mira. I need every byline on this list cross-checked against any wire that came out of the Voss apartment in the last four years. Send me back the matches by email. Don't print anything.

(beat)

Yeah. I'll be at it all night.


## S#2. Phone Call · Early Evening

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah at the same chair, the city light shifted now to early-evening blue. The headset on. The first name on the list circled in pencil. He dials.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Noah at the table, the headset on
- ECU his finger circling the first name in the printed contacts
- INSERT: laptop screen, DIANE — SENIOR EDITOR — national daily
- CU the coffee cup, still untouched

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Diane. It's Noah Keene. I've sent a packet to your secure address. A reporter on your masthead has been paid by a woman named Eileen Voss for seven years. He's holding a piece about Lena Sterling that he's planning to file by midnight. I'd like the piece pulled. I'd also like the reporter pulled. Everything you need to defend the second decision is in the packet.

DIANE (filtered through headset, calm)
I'll have it read in the next twenty minutes. If it holds, the piece doesn't run.

NOAH
Thanks, Diane. I owe you one.

DIANE
You owe me two. You'll pay later.

He ends the call. He marks a checkmark beside the first name. He looks at the list. He breathes out once.


## S#2A. Phone Calls — Compressed · Evening into Night

[MONTAGE — four beats over shifting window light]
1. Noah at the table — headset on, marking a second name with a pencil checkmark. The light at the window is now evening gold.
2. INSERT — laptop screen, a different outlet on the screen each beat. CABLE NETWORK PRODUCER. FREELANCER ABROAD. SMALL-PAPER EDITOR. PODCAST HOST.
3. Noah closing one call, immediately dialing the next, no celebration. Coffee cup still on the windowsill, untouched.
4. The light at the window has gone to full dark. Five checkmarks on the list. Three names below them not yet marked.

[VO]
NOAH (V.O.)
Eileen had planted eight stories. Each one was a phone call. I made the first one myself, in full, because I needed to hear it land. After that they were the same conversation with different names. Five of them went down in three hours. The last three were going to need me in a room.


## S#2B. Lena's Office — Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
INTERCUT — Lena's small home study. LENA at the desk in a dark sweater, hair loose, her own list on the desk in front of her — three names, separate from Noah's. The lamp the only light. Her own headset, lighter than Noah's. The first name circled.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena at her desk, headset on
- ECU her hand on a printed photo — a senior editor she has worked with for twelve years
- CU her finger on the dial button, pressing once
- INSERT — phone screen, the name connecting

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(when the line picks up, the voice on the other end an older woman she has known a long time)
Audrey. It's Lena. I'm not calling about an interview. I'm calling because your name was on a list Eileen Voss kept of editors she expected to publish next. I'd like you to read the packet Noah is sending to your office now and tell me by the morning whether the relationship she thought she had with your team is the relationship you actually have. That's all I'm asking. I trust you to look at it honestly.

AUDREY (filtered, dry)
Lena. If my name is on her list I'm going to be furious for a week. I'll have it read in an hour.

LENA
I'd rather you be furious for a week than published for a morning. Thank you, Audrey.

She ends the call. She marks one checkmark. She looks at the next two names on the list. She picks up the phone again.


## S#2C. Eileen — Holding Facility · Same Hour

[VISUAL/ACTION]
INTERCUT — a small cell in the holding facility where EILEEN has been remanded since the airfield. Concrete walls, a bunk, a small writing surface bolted to the wall, a chair. Eileen in pale institutional blue, hair already pulled back into a flat plain bun. She sits at the writing surface with a single sheet of pale lined paper and a short blunt pencil — the kind they give you to keep you from doing anything else with it. She is writing. Slow, careful, no haste.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the cell, the small writing surface, Eileen at the chair
- MCU Eileen at the writing surface, head down
- ECU the page — the salutation visible at the top: *My dearest Mara.*
- CU her hand, the pencil moving line by line

[VO]
EILEEN (V.O.)
The first thing I had taught my daughter was that a letter, written in the right voice, could reach a person no door in this country could keep me from. I had been thinking about the wording of this one for nine days. I had time. I had nothing else but time.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She finishes a paragraph. She reads it back to herself silently. She does not nod. She does not smile. She moves the pencil to the next line. Outside the cell, the corridor light moves once with a footstep that doesn't stop.


## S#3. Parking Garage — Level Three · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
An underground parking garage, fluorescent strip lights, two cars on the whole level. Noah leans against the trunk of his own car, hands in his coat pockets, a slim manila envelope under one arm. The journalist — call him REYES — fifties, raincoat over a corduroy jacket, the sort of face that has stopped being surprised by anything — comes down the ramp on foot. He stops six feet from Noah.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the empty garage level
- MCU Noah at the back of his car
- OTS Reyes approaching
- ECU the manila envelope under Noah's arm

[DIALOGUE]
REYES
Keene. I haven't seen you since the Halloran case. You said on the phone this wasn't a threat.

NOAH
It isn't a threat. I'd like you to read what's in this envelope and then I'd like to ask you a question. You don't have to answer the question tonight. I'd just like to ask it.

He hands Reyes the envelope. Reyes opens it, leans against his own car, reads three pages in the white fluorescent light without sitting down. He reads slowly, the way a man does when he already knows what's in front of him and is checking to see if it's the version he was afraid of.

REYES
(without looking up)
She paid me through a numbered company. I have known for six years that the numbered company traced back to her. I have spent six years deciding I didn't want to know.

NOAH
You can decide you do know now. Or you can decide you still don't. I would rather you decide you do. There is a woman in a hospital bed across the river who got there because the people on your story didn't decide they did.

REYES
(after a long beat, lowering the page)
What would you like me to do.

NOAH
Retract the piece you've been holding. Put your name to a written record of the relationship. Be available to testify if the federal side asks you to be. That's the whole request. I'm not going to ask for anything else.

REYES
And if I say yes.

NOAH
Then I drive home and you go upstairs and you write the retraction tonight. Tomorrow morning the office that handles your professional license calls you to confirm a few things. They will not be hard things. You'll keep your job.

REYES
(looking at the envelope, then at Noah, then back at the envelope)
All right. I'll write it tonight.

NOAH
Thank you.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Reyes closing the envelope, holding it under one arm
- CU his other hand, very steady
- OTS Noah → Reyes
- Wide of the two of them in the white light, neither one moving for a beat


## S#4. Noah's Car · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah is back in his car, parked on the upper deck of the garage now, the city visible through the windshield. He has not driven away. He is on the phone. The dashboard screen shows LENA on the call.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Noah in the driver's seat
- ECU the dashboard screen, LENA on the call
- OTS Noah → the city through the windshield
- CU his hand on the wheel, the wedding band of his own — only the wristwatch — catching the light

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
It's done. Yours and mine, eleven names between us. Two retractions filed already. The rest are going up by midweek. Reyes is writing his tonight. We can stop running.

LENA
(filtered through the dashboard)
You sound tired.

NOAH
I am tired. I'd like to come over.

LENA
Then come over. Bring food. I haven't eaten since this started.

NOAH
What do you want.

LENA
Whatever's open at this hour. Whatever you'd want. Just don't make me decide tonight.


## S#5. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's apartment, the city visible through the tall windows, the lamp on the side table the only light in the room. LENA stands at the window with her coat still on, her phone in her hand, the screen dimming and brightening as she watches it. Behind her, on the low coffee table, a folder of paperwork from Bannon's office sits closed. She has not touched it tonight.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room, Lena at the window
- MCU Lena from the side, the city behind her
- ECU her phone, the call screen showing NOAH ENDED
- OTS Lena → the city through the window

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
There is a thing my mother used to do at the end of a long week. She would stand at her own window for half an hour and not call anyone. She said the half hour at the window was the only thing that kept her honest. I never understood what she meant by that. I am standing at my window tonight not calling anyone, and I think I understand it now. It is the half hour where you decide what you spent the week protecting.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(to herself, very low, two full sentences)
He'll be here in twenty minutes. I'd better take the coat off.

She turns from the window. She unbuttons her coat. She lays it across the arm of the couch. She crosses to the kitchen, opens a cupboard, takes down two plates. Sets them on the counter. Stands with her hand on the rim of one of them for a beat.

[END HOOK]
A buzz at the door. The intercom. Lena lifts it. Noah's voice on the line. She presses the button to let him up. She crosses back to the couch, picks her coat up off the arm, folds it once, lays it across the back. She is at the door when his footsteps reach the landing. Hold on her hand on the latch as she opens the door. Hold on Noah on the other side of the threshold, paper bag in one hand, his coat over his other arm. Neither of them speaks. Cut to the kitchen — both plates already on the counter — and the closed folder on the coffee table behind them, the city light catching its edge.

Hard Cut.
